# UniSat Wallet | UniSat

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://docs.unisat.io/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://docs.unisat.io/developer-support/open-api-documentation/unisat-wallet.md)
.

UniSat Wallet API[](https://docs.unisat.io/developer-support/open-api-documentation/unisat-wallet#unisat-wallet-api)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Welcome to UniSat's Developer Documentation. This documentation is for learning to develop applications for UniSat Wallet.

Getting Started[](https://docs.unisat.io/developer-support/open-api-documentation/unisat-wallet#getting-started)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

To develop for UniSat Wallet, install UniSat Wallet on your development machine. [Download here](https://unisat.io/)
.

Once UniSat Wallet is installed and running, you should find that new browser tabs have a `window.unisat` object available in the developer console. This is how your website will interact with UniSat Wallet.

### Browser Detection[](https://docs.unisat.io/developer-support/open-api-documentation/unisat-wallet#browser-detection)

To verify if the browser is running UniSat Wallet, copy and paste the code snippet below in the developer console of your web browser:

Copy

    if (typeof window.unisat !== 'undefined') {
      console.log('UniSat Wallet is installed!');
    }

You can review the full API for the `window.unisat` object here.

### Connecting to UniSat Wallet[](https://docs.unisat.io/developer-support/open-api-documentation/unisat-wallet#connecting-to-unisat-wallet)

"Connecting" or "logging in" to UniSat Wallet effectively means "to access the user's Bitcoin account(s)".

You should **only** initiate a connection request in response to direct user action, such as clicking a button. You should **always** disable the "connect" button while the connection request is pending. You should **never** initiate a connection request on page load.

We recommend that you provide a button to allow the user to connect UniSat Wallet to your dapp. Clicking this button should call the following method:

Copy

    unisat.requestAccounts()

### Demo[](https://docs.unisat.io/developer-support/open-api-documentation/unisat-wallet#demo)

*   [Online Demo](https://demo.unisat.io/)
    
*   [Demo source code](https://github.com/unisat-wallet/unisat-web3-demo)
    

Methods[](https://docs.unisat.io/developer-support/open-api-documentation/unisat-wallet#methods)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### requestAccounts[](https://docs.unisat.io/developer-support/open-api-documentation/unisat-wallet#requestaccounts)

Copy

    unisat.requestAccounts()

Connect the current account.

**Parameters**

none

**Returns**

`Promise` returns `string[]` : Address of current account.

**Example**

Copy

    try {
      let accounts = await window.unisat.requestAccounts();
      console.log('connect success', accounts);
    } catch (e) {
      console.log('connect failed');
    }
    > connect success ['tb1qrn7tvhdf6wnh790384ahj56u0xaa0kqgautnnz']

### getAccounts[](https://docs.unisat.io/developer-support/open-api-documentation/unisat-wallet#getaccounts)

Copy

    unisat.getAccounts()

Get address of current account

**Parameters**

none

**Returns**

*   `Promise` - `string`: address of current account
    

**Example**

Copy

    try {
      let res = await window.unisat.getAccounts();
      console.log(res)
    } catch (e) {
      console.log(e);
    }
    > ["tb1qrn7tvhdf6wnh790384ahj56u0xaa0kqgautnnz"]

### getNetwork[](https://docs.unisat.io/developer-support/open-api-documentation/unisat-wallet#getnetwork)

Copy

    unisat.getNetwork()

get network

Please note that this method only supports bitcoin mainnet and bitcoin testnet. Due to the support for more networks, please switch to the **getChain** method.

**Parameters**

none

**Returns**

*   `Promise` - `string`: the network. `livenet` and `testnet`
    

**Example**

Copy

    try {
      let res = await window.unisat.getNetwork();
      console.log(res)
    } catch (e) {
      console.log(e);
    }
    
    > 0

### switchNetwork[](https://docs.unisat.io/developer-support/open-api-documentation/unisat-wallet#switchnetwork)

Copy

    unisat.switchNetwork(network)

switch network

Please note that this method only supports bitcoin mainnet and bitcoin testnet. Due to the support for more networks, please switch to the **switchChain** method.

**Parameters**

*   `network` - `string`: the network. `livenet` and `testnet`
    

**Returns**

none

**Example**

Copy

    try {
      let res = await window.unisat.switchNetwork("livenet");
      console.log(res)
    } catch (e) {
      console.log(e);
    }
    
    > 0

### getChain[](https://docs.unisat.io/developer-support/open-api-documentation/unisat-wallet#getchain)

Copy

    unisat.getChain()

get chain

_( Requires extension version greater than 1.4.0 )_

[Supported Chains](https://docs.unisat.io/developer-support/open-api-documentation/unisat-wallet/supported-chains)

**Parameters**

none

**Returns**

*   `Promise` - `Object`:
    
    *   `enum` - `string` : the enum of chain
        
    *   `name` - `string` : the name of chain
        
    *   `network` - `string` : `livenet` or `testnet`
        
    

**Example**

Copy

    try {
      let res = await window.unisat.getChain();
      console.log(res)
    } catch (e) {
      console.log(e);
    }
    
    >  {enum: 'BITCOIN_MAINNET', name: 'Bitcoin Mainnet', network: 'livenet'}

### switchChain[](https://docs.unisat.io/developer-support/open-api-documentation/unisat-wallet#switchchain)

Copy

    unisat.switchChain(chain)

switch chain

_( Requires extension version greater than 1.4.0 )_

**Parameters**

*   `chain` - `string`: the chain. `BITCOIN_MAINNET` or `BITCOIN_TESTNET` or `FRACTAL_BITCOIN_MAINNET`
    

**Returns**

*   `Promise` - `Object`:
    
    *   `enum` - `string` : the enum of chain
        
    *   `name` - `string` : the name of chain
        
    *   `network` - `string` : `livenet` or `testnet`
        
    

**Example**

Copy

    try {
      let res = await window.unisat.switchChain("BITCOIN_MAINNET");
      console.log(res)
    } catch (e) {
      console.log(e);
    }
    
    > {enum: 'BITCOIN_MAINNET', name: 'Bitcoin Mainnet', network: 'livenet'}

### getPublicKey[](https://docs.unisat.io/developer-support/open-api-documentation/unisat-wallet#getpublickey)

Copy

    unisat.getPublicKey()

Get publicKey of current account.

**Parameters**

none

**Returns**

*   `Promise` - `string`: publicKey
    

**Example**

Copy

    try {
      let res = await window.unisat.getPublicKey();
      console.log(res)
    } catch (e) {
      console.log(e);
    }
    > 03cbaedc26f03fd3ba02fc936f338e980c9e2172c5e23128877ed46827e935296f

### getBalance[](https://docs.unisat.io/developer-support/open-api-documentation/unisat-wallet#getbalance)

Copy

    unisat.getBalance()

Get BTC balance

**Parameters**

none

**Returns**

*   `Promise` - `Object`:
    
    *   `confirmed` - `number` : the confirmed satoshis
        
    *   `unconfirmed` - `number` : the unconfirmed satoshis
        
    *   `total` - `number` : the total satoshis
        
    

**Example**

Copy

    try {
      let res = await window.unisat.getBalance();
      console.log(res)
    } catch (e) {
      console.log(e);
    }
    
    > {
        "confirmed":0,
        "unconfirmed":100000,
        "total":100000
      }

### getInscriptions[](https://docs.unisat.io/developer-support/open-api-documentation/unisat-wallet#getinscriptions)

Copy

    unisat.getInscriptions(cursor,size)

List inscriptions of current account

**Parameters**

none

**Returns**

*   `Promise` - `Object`:
    
    *   `total` - `number` : the total count
        
    *   `list` - `Object[]` :
        
    *   `inscriptionId` - `string` : the id of inscription.
        
    *   `inscriptionNumber` - `string` : the number of inscription.
        
    *   `address` - `string` : the address of inscription.
        
    *   `outputValue` - `string` : the output value of inscription.
        
    *   `content` - `string` : the content url of inscription.
        
    *   `contentLength` - `string` : the content length of inscription.
        
    *   `contentType` - `number` : the content type of inscription.
        
    *   `preview` - `number` : the preview link
        
    *   `timestamp` - `number` : the blocktime of inscription.
        
    *   `offset` - `number` : the offset of inscription.
        
    *   `genesisTransaction` - `string` : the txid of genesis transaction
        
    *   `location` - `string` : the txid and vout of current location
        
    

**Example**

Copy

    try {
      let res = await window.unisat.getInscriptions(0,10);
      console.log(res)
    } catch (e) {
      console.log(e);
    }
    
    > {
      "total":10,
      "list":[\
        {\
          inscriptionId: '6037b17df2f48cf87f6b6e6ff89af416f6f21dd3d3bc9f1832fb1ff560037531i0',\
          inscriptionNumber: 959941,\
          address: 'bc1q8h8s4zd9y0lkrx334aqnj4ykqs220ss735a3gh',\
          outputValue: 546,\
          preview: 'https://ordinals.com/preview/6037b17df2f48cf87f6b6e6ff89af416f6f21dd3d3bc9f1832fb1ff560037531i0',\
          content: 'https://ordinals.com/content/6037b17df2f48cf87f6b6e6ff89af416f6f21dd3d3bc9f1832fb1ff560037531i0',\
          contentLength: 53,\
          contentType: 'text/plain;charset=utf-8',\
          timestamp: 1680865285,\
          genesisTransaction: '6037b17df2f48cf87f6b6e6ff89af416f6f21dd3d3bc9f1832fb1ff560037531',\
          location: '6037b17df2f48cf87f6b6e6ff89af416f6f21dd3d3bc9f1832fb1ff560037531:0:0',\
          output: '6037b17df2f48cf87f6b6e6ff89af416f6f21dd3d3bc9f1832fb1ff560037531:0',\
          offset: 0\
        }\
      ]
    }

### sendBitcoin[](https://docs.unisat.io/developer-support/open-api-documentation/unisat-wallet#sendbitcoin)

Copy

    unisat.sendBitcoin(toAddress, satoshis, options)

Send BTC

**Parameters**

*   `toAddress` - `string`: the address to send
    
*   `satoshis` - `number`: the satoshis to send
    
*   `options` - `object`: (optional)
    
    *   `feeRate` - `number`: the network fee rate
        
    *   `memo` - `string`: (optional) The data content of OP\_RETURN, which needs to be in UTF-8 or HEX encoded format.
        
    *   `memos` - `string[]`: (optional) The data content of OP\_RETURN, which needs to be in UTF-8 or HEX encoded format, provided in the form of an array.
        
    

**Returns**

*   `Promise` - `string`: txid
    

**Example**

Copy

    try {
      let txid = await window.unisat.sendBitcoin("tb1qrn7tvhdf6wnh790384ahj56u0xaa0kqgautnnz",1000);
      console.log(txid)
    } catch (e) {
      console.log(e);
    }

### signMessage[](https://docs.unisat.io/developer-support/open-api-documentation/unisat-wallet#signmessage)

Copy

    unisat.signMessage(msg[, type])

sign message

**Parameters**

*   `msg` - `string`: a string to sign
    
*   `type` - `string`: (Optional) "ecdsa" | "bip322-simple". default is "ecdsa"
    

**Returns**

*   `Promise` - `string`: the signature.
    

**Example**

Copy

    // sign by ecdsa
    try {
      let res = await window.unisat.signMessage("abcdefghijk123456789");
      console.log(res)
    } catch (e) {
      console.log(e);
    }
    
    > G+LrYa7T5dUMDgQduAErw+i6ebK4GqTXYVWIDM+snYk7Yc6LdPitmaqM6j+iJOeID1CsMXOJFpVopvPiHBdulkE=
    
    // verify by ecdsa
    import { verifyMessage } from "@unisat/wallet-utils";
    const pubkey = "026887958bcc4cb6f8c04ea49260f0d10e312c41baf485252953b14724db552aac";
    const message = "abcdefghijk123456789";
    const signature = "G+LrYa7T5dUMDgQduAErw+i6ebK4GqTXYVWIDM+snYk7Yc6LdPitmaqM6j+iJOeID1CsMXOJFpVopvPiHBdulkE=";
    const result = verifyMessage(pubkey,message,signature);
    console.log(result);
    
    > true
    
    
    // sign by bip322-simple
    try {
      let res = await window.unisat.signMessage("abcdefghijk123456789","bip322-simple");
      console.log(res)
    } catch (e) {
      console.log(e);
    }
    
    > AkcwRAIgeHUcjr0jODaR7GMM8cenWnIj0MYdGmmrpGyMoryNSkgCICzVXWrLIKKp5cFtaCTErY7FGNXTFe6kuEofl4G+Vi5wASECaIeVi8xMtvjATqSSYPDRDjEsQbr0hSUpU7FHJNtVKqw=

### signPsbt[](https://docs.unisat.io/developer-support/open-api-documentation/unisat-wallet#signpsbt)

Copy

    unisat.signPsbt(psbtHex[, options])

Sign PSBT

This method will traverse all inputs that match the current address to sign.

**Parameters**

*   `psbtHex` - `string`: the hex string of psbt to sign
    
*   options
    
    *   `autoFinalized` - `boolean`: whether finalize psbt after signing, default is true
        
    *   `toSignInputs` - `array`:
        
        *   `index` - `number`: which input to sign
            
        *   `address` - `string`: (at least specify either an address or a publicKey) Which corresponding private key to use for signing
            
        *   `publicKey` - `string`: (at least specify either an address or a publicKey) Which corresponding private key to use for signing
            
        *   `sighashTypes` \- `number[]`: (optionals) sighashTypes
            
        *   `disableTweakSigner` - `boolean` :(optionals) Default value is false. Setting it true allows the use of the original private key when signing taproot inputs.
            
        *   `useTweakedSigner` - `boolean` :(optionals) . By setting useTweakedSigner, you can forcibly decide whether or not to use tweakedSigner. It has a higher priority than disableTweakSigner.
            
        
    

**Returns**

*   `Promise` - `string`: the hex string of signed psbt
    

**Example**

Copy

    try {
      let res = await window.unisat.signPsbt(
        "70736274ff01007d....",
        {
            autoFinalized:false,
            toSignInputs:[\
              {\
                index: 0,\
                address: "tb1q8h8....mjxzny",\
              },\
              {\
                index: 1,\
                publicKey: "tb1q8h8....mjxzny",\
                sighashTypes: [1]\
              },\
              {\
                index: 2,\
                publicKey: "02062...8779693f",\
              }\
            ]
        }
      );
      console.log(res)
    } catch (e) {
      console.log(e);
    }
    
    unisat.signPsbt("xxxxxxxx",{toSignInputs:[{index:0,publicKey:"xxxxxx",disableTweakSigner:true}],autoFinalized:false})
    

### signPsbts[](https://docs.unisat.io/developer-support/open-api-documentation/unisat-wallet#signpsbts)

Copy

    unisat.signPsbts(psbtHexs[, options])

Sign Multiple PSBTs at once

This method will traverse all inputs that match the current address to sign.

**Parameters**

*   `psbtHexs` - `string[]`: the hex strings of psbt to sign
    
*   `options` - `object`\[\]: the options of signing psbt
    
    *   `autoFinalized` - `boolean`: whether finalize psbt after signing, default is true
        
    *   `toSignInputs` - `array`:
        
        *   `index` - `number`: which input to sign
            
        *   `address` - `string`: (at least specify either an address or a publicKey) Which corresponding private key to use for signing
            
        *   `publicKey` - `string`: (at least specify either an address or a publicKey) Which corresponding private key to use for signing
            
        *   `sighashTypes` \- `number[]`: (optionals) sighashTypes
            
        *   `useTweakedSigner` - `boolean` :(optionals) . By setting useTweakedSigner, you can forcibly decide whether or not to use tweakedSigner. It has a higher priority than disableTweakSigner.
            
        
    

**Returns**

*   `Promise` - `string[]`: the hex strings of signed psbt
    

**Example**

Copy

    try {
      let res = await window.unisat.signPsbts(["70736274ff01007d...","70736274ff01007d..."]);
      console.log(res)
    } catch (e) {
      console.log(e);
    }

Events[](https://docs.unisat.io/developer-support/open-api-documentation/unisat-wallet#events)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### accountsChanged[](https://docs.unisat.io/developer-support/open-api-documentation/unisat-wallet#accountschanged)

Copy

    unisat.on('accountsChanged', handler: (accounts: Array<string>) => void);
    unisat.removeListener('accountsChanged', handler: (accounts: Array<string>) => void);

The `accountsChanged` will be emitted whenever the user's exposed account address changes.

### networkChanged[](https://docs.unisat.io/developer-support/open-api-documentation/unisat-wallet#networkchanged)

Copy

    unisat.on('networkChanged', handler: (network: string) => void);
    unisat.removeListener('networkChanged', handler: (network: string) => void);

The `networkChanged` will be emitted whenever the user's network changes.

# Unknown

\> For the complete documentation index, see \[llms.txt\](https://docs.unisat.io/llms.txt). Markdown versions of documentation pages are available by appending \`.md\` to page URLs; this page is available as \[Markdown\](https://docs.unisat.io/developer-support/open-api-documentation/unisat-wallet.md). # UniSat Wallet ## UniSat Wallet API Welcome to UniSat's Developer Documentation. This documentation is for learning to develop applications for UniSat Wallet. ## Getting Started To develop for UniSat Wallet, install UniSat Wallet on your development machine. \[Download here\](https://unisat.io/). Once UniSat Wallet is installed and running, you should find that new browser tabs have a \`window.unisat\` object available in the developer console. This is how your website will interact with UniSat Wallet. ### Browser Detection To verify if the browser is running UniSat Wallet, copy and paste the code snippet below in the developer console of your web browser: \`\`\`javascript if (typeof window.unisat !== 'undefined') { console.log('UniSat Wallet is installed!'); } \`\`\` You can review the full API for the \`window.unisat\` object here. ### Connecting to UniSat Wallet "Connecting" or "logging in" to UniSat Wallet effectively means "to access the user's Bitcoin account(s)". You should \*\*only\*\* initiate a connection request in response to direct user action, such as clicking a button. You should \*\*always\*\* disable the "connect" button while the connection request is pending. You should \*\*never\*\* initiate a connection request on page load. We recommend that you provide a button to allow the user to connect UniSat Wallet to your dapp. Clicking this button should call the following method: \`\`\`javascript unisat.requestAccounts() \`\`\` ### Demo \* \[Online Demo\](https://demo.unisat.io) \* \[Demo source code\](https://github.com/unisat-wallet/unisat-web3-demo) ## Methods ### requestAccounts \`\`\`typescript unisat.requestAccounts() \`\`\` Connect the current account. \*\*Parameters\*\* none \*\*Returns\*\* \`Promise\` returns \`string\[\]\` : Address of current account. \*\*Example\*\* \`\`\`typescript try { let accounts = await window.unisat.requestAccounts(); console.log('connect success', accounts); } catch (e) { console.log('connect failed'); } > connect success \['tb1qrn7tvhdf6wnh790384ahj56u0xaa0kqgautnnz'\] \`\`\` ### getAccounts \`\`\`typescript unisat.getAccounts() \`\`\` Get address of current account \*\*Parameters\*\* none \*\*Returns\*\* \* \`Promise\` - \`string\`: address of current account \*\*Example\*\* \`\`\`typescript try { let res = await window.unisat.getAccounts(); console.log(res) } catch (e) { console.log(e); } > \["tb1qrn7tvhdf6wnh790384ahj56u0xaa0kqgautnnz"\] \`\`\` ### getNetwork \`\`\`typescript unisat.getNetwork() \`\`\` get network {% hint style="warning" %} Please note that this method only supports bitcoin mainnet and bitcoin testnet. Due to the support for more networks, please switch to the \*\*getChain\*\* method. {% endhint %} \*\*Parameters\*\* none \*\*Returns\*\* \* \`Promise\` - \`string\`: the network. \`livenet\` and \`testnet\` \*\*Example\*\* \`\`\`typescript try { let res = await window.unisat.getNetwork(); console.log(res) } catch (e) { console.log(e); } > 0 \`\`\` ### switchNetwork \`\`\`typescript unisat.switchNetwork(network) \`\`\` switch network {% hint style="warning" %} Please note that this method only supports bitcoin mainnet and bitcoin testnet. Due to the support for more networks, please switch to the \*\*switchChain\*\* method. {% endhint %} \*\*Parameters\*\* \* \`network\` - \`string\`: the network. \`livenet\` and \`testnet\` \*\*Returns\*\* none \*\*Example\*\* \`\`\`typescript try { let res = await window.unisat.switchNetwork("livenet"); console.log(res) } catch (e) { console.log(e); } > 0 \`\`\` ### getChain \`\`\`typescript unisat.getChain() \`\`\` get chain \*( Requires extension version greater than 1.4.0 )\* {% content-ref url="/pages/LACJAW3GKcae50U6gjg5" %} \[Supported Chains\](/developer-support/open-api-documentation/unisat-wallet/supported-chains.md) {% endcontent-ref %} \*\*Parameters\*\* none \*\*Returns\*\* \* \`Promise\` - \`Object\`: \* \`enum\` - \`string\` : the enum of chain \* \`name\` - \`string\` : the name of chain \* \`network\` - \`string\` : \`livenet\` or \`testnet\` \*\*Example\*\* \`\`\`typescript try { let res = await window.unisat.getChain(); console.log(res) } catch (e) { console.log(e); } > {enum: 'BITCOIN\_MAINNET', name: 'Bitcoin Mainnet', network: 'livenet'} \`\`\` ### switchChain \`\`\`typescript unisat.switchChain(chain) \`\`\` switch chain \*( Requires extension version greater than 1.4.0 )\* \*\*Parameters\*\* \* \`chain\` - \`string\`: the chain. \`BITCOIN\_MAINNET\` or \`BITCOIN\_TESTNET\` or \`FRACTAL\_BITCOIN\_MAINNET\` \*\*Returns\*\* \* \`Promise\` - \`Object\`: \* \`enum\` - \`string\` : the enum of chain \* \`name\` - \`string\` : the name of chain \* \`network\` - \`string\` : \`livenet\` or \`testnet\` \*\*Example\*\* \`\`\`typescript try { let res = await window.unisat.switchChain("BITCOIN\_MAINNET"); console.log(res) } catch (e) { console.log(e); } > {enum: 'BITCOIN\_MAINNET', name: 'Bitcoin Mainnet', network: 'livenet'} \`\`\` ### getPublicKey \`\`\`typescript unisat.getPublicKey() \`\`\` Get publicKey of current account. \*\*Parameters\*\* none \*\*Returns\*\* \* \`Promise\` - \`string\`: publicKey \*\*Example\*\* \`\`\`typescript try { let res = await window.unisat.getPublicKey(); console.log(res) } catch (e) { console.log(e); } > 03cbaedc26f03fd3ba02fc936f338e980c9e2172c5e23128877ed46827e935296f \`\`\` ### getBalance \`\`\`typescript unisat.getBalance() \`\`\` Get BTC balance \*\*Parameters\*\* none \*\*Returns\*\* \* \`Promise\` - \`Object\`: \* \`confirmed\` - \`number\` : the confirmed satoshis \* \`unconfirmed\` - \`number\` : the unconfirmed satoshis \* \`total\` - \`number\` : the total satoshis \*\*Example\*\* \`\`\`typescript try { let res = await window.unisat.getBalance(); console.log(res) } catch (e) { console.log(e); } > { "confirmed":0, "unconfirmed":100000, "total":100000 } \`\`\` ### getInscriptions \`\`\`typescript unisat.getInscriptions(cursor,size) \`\`\` List inscriptions of current account \*\*Parameters\*\* none \*\*Returns\*\* \* \`Promise\` - \`Object\`: \* \`total\` - \`number\` : the total count \* \`list\` - \`Object\[\]\` : \* \`inscriptionId\` - \`string\` : the id of inscription. \* \`inscriptionNumber\` - \`string\` : the number of inscription. \* \`address\` - \`string\` : the address of inscription. \* \`outputValue\` - \`string\` : the output value of inscription. \* \`content\` - \`string\` : the content url of inscription. \* \`contentLength\` - \`string\` : the content length of inscription. \* \`contentType\` - \`number\` : the content type of inscription. \* \`preview\` - \`number\` : the preview link \* \`timestamp\` - \`number\` : the blocktime of inscription. \* \`offset\` - \`number\` : the offset of inscription. \* \`genesisTransaction\` - \`string\` : the txid of genesis transaction \* \`location\` - \`string\` : the txid and vout of current location \*\*Example\*\* \`\`\`typescript try { let res = await window.unisat.getInscriptions(0,10); console.log(res) } catch (e) { console.log(e); } > { "total":10, "list":\[ { inscriptionId: '6037b17df2f48cf87f6b6e6ff89af416f6f21dd3d3bc9f1832fb1ff560037531i0', inscriptionNumber: 959941, address: 'bc1q8h8s4zd9y0lkrx334aqnj4ykqs220ss735a3gh', outputValue: 546, preview: 'https://ordinals.com/preview/6037b17df2f48cf87f6b6e6ff89af416f6f21dd3d3bc9f1832fb1ff560037531i0', content: 'https://ordinals.com/content/6037b17df2f48cf87f6b6e6ff89af416f6f21dd3d3bc9f1832fb1ff560037531i0', contentLength: 53, contentType: 'text/plain;charset=utf-8', timestamp: 1680865285, genesisTransaction: '6037b17df2f48cf87f6b6e6ff89af416f6f21dd3d3bc9f1832fb1ff560037531', location: '6037b17df2f48cf87f6b6e6ff89af416f6f21dd3d3bc9f1832fb1ff560037531:0:0', output: '6037b17df2f48cf87f6b6e6ff89af416f6f21dd3d3bc9f1832fb1ff560037531:0', offset: 0 } \] } \`\`\` ### sendBitcoin \`\`\`typescript unisat.sendBitcoin(toAddress, satoshis, options) \`\`\` Send BTC \*\*Parameters\*\* \* \`toAddress\` - \`string\`: the address to send \* \`satoshis\` - \`number\`: the satoshis to send \* \`options\` - \`object\`: (optional) \* \`feeRate\` - \`number\`: the network fee rate \* \`memo\` - \`string\`: (optional) The data content of OP\\\_RETURN, which needs to be in UTF-8 or HEX encoded format. \* \`memos\` - \`string\[\]\`: (optional) The data content of OP\\\_RETURN, which needs to be in UTF-8 or HEX encoded format, provided in the form of an array. \*\*Returns\*\* \* \`Promise\` - \`string\`: txid \*\*Example\*\* \`\`\`typescript try { let txid = await window.unisat.sendBitcoin("tb1qrn7tvhdf6wnh790384ahj56u0xaa0kqgautnnz",1000); console.log(txid) } catch (e) { console.log(e); } \`\`\` ### sendRunes \`\`\`typescript unisat.sendRunes(address, runeid, amount, options) \`\`\` Send Runes \*\*Parameters\*\* \* \`address\` - \`string\`: the receiver address. \* \`runeid\` - \`string\`: the runeid \* \`amount\` - \`string\`: the amount to send \* \`options\` - \`Object\`: (optional) \* \`feeRate\` - \`number\`: the network fee rate \*\*Returns\*\* \* \`Promise\` - \`Object\`: \* \`txid\` - \`string\` : the txid \*\*Example\*\* \`\`\`typescript try { let txid = await window.unisat.sendRunes("tb1q8h8s4zd9y0lkrx334aqnj4ykqs220ss7mjxzny","63104:1",10,{feeRate:15}); console.log(txid) } catch (e) { console.log(e); } \`\`\` ### sendInscription \`\`\`typescript unisat.sendInscription(address, inscriptionId, options) \`\`\` Send Inscription \*\*Parameters\*\* \* \`address\` - \`string\`: the receiver address. \* \`inscriptionId\` - \`string\`: the id of Inscription \* \`options\` - \`Object\`: (optional) \* \`feeRate\` - \`number\`: the network fee rate \*\*Returns\*\* \* \`Promise\` - \`Object\`: \* \`txid\` - \`string\` : the txid \*\*Example\*\* \`\`\`typescript try { let txid = await window.unisat.sendInscription("tb1q8h8s4zd9y0lkrx334aqnj4ykqs220ss7mjxzny","e9b86a063d78cc8a1ed17d291703bcc95bcd521e087ab0c7f1621c9c607def1ai0",{feeRate:15}); console.log("send Inscription 204 to tb1q8h8s4zd9y0lkrx334aqnj4ykqs220ss7mjxzny",txid) } catch (e) { console.log(e); } \`\`\` ### inscribeTransfer \`\`\`typescript unisat.inscribeTransfer(ticker, amount) \`\`\` Inscribe BRC-20 TRANSFER Inscription \*\*Parameters\*\* \* \`ticker\` - \`string\`: BRC-20 ticker \* \`amount\` - \`string\`: the amount to inscribe \*\*Returns\*\* \* \`Promise\` - \`void\`: \*\*Example\*\* \`\`\`typescript window.unisat.inscribeTransfer("ordi","100"); \`\`\` ### signMessage \`\`\`typescript unisat.signMessage(msg\[, type\]) \`\`\` sign message \*\*Parameters\*\* \* \`msg\` - \`string\`: a string to sign \* \`type\` - \`string\`: (Optional) "ecdsa" | "bip322-simple". default is "ecdsa" \*\*Returns\*\* \* \`Promise\` - \`string\`: the signature. \*\*Example\*\* \`\`\`typescript // sign by ecdsa try { let res = await window.unisat.signMessage("abcdefghijk123456789"); console.log(res) } catch (e) { console.log(e); } > G+LrYa7T5dUMDgQduAErw+i6ebK4GqTXYVWIDM+snYk7Yc6LdPitmaqM6j+iJOeID1CsMXOJFpVopvPiHBdulkE= // verify by ecdsa import { verifyMessage } from "@unisat/wallet-utils"; const pubkey = "026887958bcc4cb6f8c04ea49260f0d10e312c41baf485252953b14724db552aac"; const message = "abcdefghijk123456789"; const signature = "G+LrYa7T5dUMDgQduAErw+i6ebK4GqTXYVWIDM+snYk7Yc6LdPitmaqM6j+iJOeID1CsMXOJFpVopvPiHBdulkE="; const result = verifyMessage(pubkey,message,signature); console.log(result); > true // sign by bip322-simple try { let res = await window.unisat.signMessage("abcdefghijk123456789","bip322-simple"); console.log(res) } catch (e) { console.log(e); } > AkcwRAIgeHUcjr0jODaR7GMM8cenWnIj0MYdGmmrpGyMoryNSkgCICzVXWrLIKKp5cFtaCTErY7FGNXTFe6kuEofl4G+Vi5wASECaIeVi8xMtvjATqSSYPDRDjEsQbr0hSUpU7FHJNtVKqw= \`\`\` ### pushTx \`\`\`typescript unisat.pushTx(options) \`\`\` Push Transaction \*\*Parameters\*\* \* \`options\` - \`Object\`: \* \`rawtx\` - \`string\`: rawtx to push \*\*Returns\*\* \* \`Promise\` - \`string\`: txid \*\*Example\*\* \`\`\`typescript try { let txid = await window.unisat.pushTx({ rawtx:"0200000000010135bd7d..." }); console.log(txid) } catch (e) { console.log(e); } \`\`\` ### signPsbt \`\`\`typescript unisat.signPsbt(psbtHex\[, options\]) \`\`\` Sign PSBT This method will traverse all inputs that match the current address to sign. \*\*Parameters\*\* \* \`psbtHex\` - \`string\`: the hex string of psbt to sign \* options \* \`autoFinalized\` - \`boolean\`: whether finalize psbt after signing, default is true \* \`toSignInputs\` - \`array\`: \* \`index\` - \`number\`: which input to sign \* \`address\` - \`string\`: (at least specify either an address or a publicKey) Which corresponding private key to use for signing \* \`publicKey\` - \`string\`: (at least specify either an address or a publicKey) Which corresponding private key to use for signing \* \`sighashTypes\` - \`number\[\]\`: (optionals) sighashTypes \* \`disableTweakSigner\` - \`boolean\` :(optionals) Default value is false. Setting it true allows the use of the original private key when signing taproot inputs. \* \`useTweakedSigner\` - \`boolean\` :(optionals) . By setting useTweakedSigner, you can forcibly decide whether or not to use tweakedSigner. It has a higher priority than disableTweakSigner. \*\*Returns\*\* \* \`Promise\` - \`string\`: the hex string of signed psbt \*\*Example\*\* \`\`\`typescript try { let res = await window.unisat.signPsbt( "70736274ff01007d....", { autoFinalized:false, toSignInputs:\[ { index: 0, address: "tb1q8h8....mjxzny", }, { index: 1, publicKey: "tb1q8h8....mjxzny", sighashTypes: \[1\] }, { index: 2, publicKey: "02062...8779693f", } \] } ); console.log(res) } catch (e) { console.log(e); } unisat.signPsbt("xxxxxxxx",{toSignInputs:\[{index:0,publicKey:"xxxxxx",disableTweakSigner:true}\],autoFinalized:false}) \`\`\` ### signPsbts \`\`\`typescript unisat.signPsbts(psbtHexs\[, options\]) \`\`\` Sign Multiple PSBTs at once This method will traverse all inputs that match the current address to sign. \*\*Parameters\*\* \* \`psbtHexs\` - \`string\[\]\`: the hex strings of psbt to sign \* \`options\` - \`object\`\\\[\]: the options of signing psbt \* \`autoFinalized\` - \`boolean\`: whether finalize psbt after signing, default is true \* \`toSignInputs\` - \`array\`: \* \`index\` - \`number\`: which input to sign \* \`address\` - \`string\`: (at least specify either an address or a publicKey) Which corresponding private key to use for signing \* \`publicKey\` - \`string\`: (at least specify either an address or a publicKey) Which corresponding private key to use for signing \* \`sighashTypes\` - \`number\[\]\`: (optionals) sighashTypes \* \`useTweakedSigner\` - \`boolean\` :(optionals) . By setting useTweakedSigner, you can forcibly decide whether or not to use tweakedSigner. It has a higher priority than disableTweakSigner. \*\*Returns\*\* \* \`Promise\` - \`string\[\]\`: the hex strings of signed psbt \*\*Example\*\* \`\`\`typescript try { let res = await window.unisat.signPsbts(\["70736274ff01007d...","70736274ff01007d..."\]); console.log(res) } catch (e) { console.log(e); } \`\`\` ### pushPsbt \`\`\`typescript unisat.pushPsbt(psbtHex) \`\`\` Push transaction \*\*Parameters\*\* \* \`psbtHex\` - \`string\`: the hex string of psbt to push \*\*Returns\*\* \* \`Promise\` - \`string\`: txid \*\*Example\*\* \`\`\`typescript try { let res = await window.unisat.pushPsbt("70736274ff01007d...."); console.log(res) } catch (e) { console.log(e); } \`\`\` ## Events ### accountsChanged \`\`\`typescript unisat.on('accountsChanged', handler: (accounts: Array) => void); unisat.removeListener('accountsChanged', handler: (accounts: Array) => void); \`\`\` The \`accountsChanged\` will be emitted whenever the user's exposed account address changes. ### networkChanged \`\`\`typescript unisat.on('networkChanged', handler: (network: string) => void); unisat.removeListener('networkChanged', handler: (network: string) => void); \`\`\` The \`networkChanged\` will be emitted whenever the user's network changes.

# Unknown

\> For the complete documentation index, see \[llms.txt\](https://docs.unisat.io/llms.txt). Markdown versions of documentation pages are available by appending \`.md\` to page URLs; this page is available as \[Markdown\](https://docs.unisat.io/welcome/unisat-work-priorities/unisat-2025-q2-work-priorities.md). # UniSat 2025 Q2 Work priorities \*\*To the UniSat Community,\*\* At the start of this year, we shared our \[\*\*2025 Work Priorities Overview\*\*\](https://docs.unisat.io/home/production-roadmap/overview-of-unisat-2025-work-priorities) to keep you informed—and we’re thrilled by how well it’s helped align our efforts with your expectations. Today, we’re excited to provide an update on our progress in 2025 so far and our plans for Q2. We’ve never stopped building, and we’re hard at work on countless exciting initiatives we can’t wait to unveil. \*\*\* ## Our Focus for Q2 2025 \*\*Elevating Your Bitcoin Trading Experience\*\* \* InSwap: The first open-sourced brc-20 DEX built natively on Bitcoin and Fractal \* UniHexa: A hybrid trading engine for Bitcoin assets \*\*Protocol Upgrade and Indexing Improvements\*\* \* brc-20 Single-step transfer \* UniScan: The one explorer for all your Bitcoin needs. \*\*Enhancing the Security and Management of Your Assets\*\* \* UniSat Wallet 2.0: Home for Your Bitcoin Assets \*\*A Step towards a more global and inclusive community\*\* \* Multilingual support for InSwap and UniSat Wallet \*\*Decentralized infrastructure for an open finance system on Bitcoin\*\* \* Decentralized indexing services \*\*UniSat’s practical approach to innovation\*\* \* Research-Driven, Decentralized, Open-Minded \* Execution Over Crafted Narratives \* Multitasking as a trusted service provider \*\*\* ### Elevating Your Bitcoin Trading Experience #### InSwap: the first open-sourced brc-20 DEX Liquidity has been a longstanding issue with brc-20 owing to orderbook marketplace being the most adopted avenue of trading, and having to wait block confirmations on Bitcoin which can take up to 30-40 mins. InSwap presents an innovative solution as the first b\*\*rc-20\*\* AMM \*\*DEX, allowing users to swap, provide liquidity and earn brc-20 easily on Bitcoin and Fractal.\*\* The automated market maker (AMM) model enables brc-20 trades to always be executed with an algorithmically determined price, without needing a counterparty. It is powered by a decentralized swap module that aggregates transactions in batches using a novel sequencer which enables: \* Instant swaps without waiting for block confirmation \* Large volumes to be processed simultaneously \* Robust decentralization and security InSwap mainnet has been live since September 2024, we shipped several key updates in 2025 so far: ✅ Integrated wrap: easier and faster wrap into swap module ✅ New domain and multilingual support: and languages like traditional chinese ✅ UI cleaning: Charts and pool info ✅ Runes swap (wrapped as a token brc-20) has been successfully implemented using SUSD and we plan to roll out more assets later in Q2. 🎡 LPfest: provide liquidity for trading pairs and get a share service fee and additional rewards in LPfest.

![](https://3523236551-files.gitbook.io/~/files/v0/b/gitbook-x-prod.appspot.com/o/spaces%2FJ4NHAHIVnWQiEecvs1By%2Fuploads%2FHDJJcTsoKM3Z67DKhoGu%2FScreenshot%202025-04-29%20at%2017.07.09%20(1).png?alt=media&token=9678b9c2-5a00-4954-88ae-dd1694c00fe1)

![](https://3523236551-files.gitbook.io/~/files/v0/b/gitbook-x-prod.appspot.com/o/spaces%2FJ4NHAHIVnWQiEecvs1By%2Fuploads%2FSVu4OsvDFlt2jlgntuB1%2FScreenshot%202025-05-02%20at%2002.54.51.png?alt=media&token=b3ab05cd-65c7-4936-9dac-1ca61a3f42ff)

What we planned for Q2: ✅ Regular LPfest epochs to boost liquidity rewards ✅ Better visualization of activities in InSwap and search feature ✅ More experimentation on how to improve trade routing and user onboarding \\ 🟠 Updates： \* Hosted multiple LPfest Epoch events: Epoch 3, 3.1, 4, and 4.1 successfully completed. \* Run several InSwap trading events with community partners. \* Feature upgrades: \* Added LP Burn feature \* Introduced One-click sign for swaps \* Enabled Custom fee rates for wrap operations \* Added Bulk Send for brc-20 transfers to multiple addresses \*\*\* #### brc-20 Swap module on Bitcoin InSwap will be deployed on Bitcoin mainnet pending implementation of the brc-20 swap module on Bitcoin, which requires brc-20 protocol upgrade and unilateral approval from all relevant parties. Therefore, we chose to deploy on Fractal first. ✅ In Q1, we included two additional implementations of the swap module on Fractal to ensure maximum reliability. Once brc-20 upgrade is approved, we will aim to launch InSwap on Bitcoin testnet by Q2 2025. This means that you will have the option to either roll up your transactions onto Bitcoin mainnet when trading assets on Bitcoin, or roll up transactions onto Fractal for lower roll up fees.\\ \\ 🟠 Updates: \\ We are currently waiting for the brc-20 upgrade to be approved and will continue to keep the community updated as progress unfolds. \*\*\* #### \*\*brc-20: Single-step transfer\*\* In the plainest sense, single-step transfer means you no longer need to inscribe a ‘transfer’ inscription and wait for block confirmations before sending brc-20! Our progress in 2025 so far: ✅ UniSat developed the solution to combine brc-20 transfer into a single step. We have deployed this upgrade on Fractal testnet and within UniSat inscribe and UniSat Wallet. ✅ We are slightly behind the announced schedule as this requires us to work closely with other indexers. The good news is we have completed development of the indexer implementations for the other main brc-20 indexers OKX and OPI, and we’re working with them to integrate. What we planned for Q2: ✅ Once sufficiently tested, \*\*Single-step transfer\*\* will be deployed on Fractal mainnet by Q2 2025. ✅ We will work with other indexers and brc-20 maintainers to push for its implementation on Bitcoin mainnet. If you enjoyed the experience of InSwap and single-step transfer and would like to see it on Bitcoin mainnet. Please let us hear your voice and support us in pushing this forward!

![](https://3523236551-files.gitbook.io/~/files/v0/b/gitbook-x-prod.appspot.com/o/spaces%2FJ4NHAHIVnWQiEecvs1By%2Fuploads%2FRFAQpdEXPLOozuZu7Vif%2F3.png?alt=media&token=ceab332d-600f-46d1-b740-1212d1800f44)

\\ 🟠 Updates: \\ ✅ brc-20 Single-Step Transfer is now live on Fractal Mainnet and we will closely monitor its performance on Fractal network and continue to advocate for its deployment on Bitcoin mainnet. \*\*\* #### UniHexa: the next-gen hybrid trading engine for Bitcoin assets UniHexa brings you the best of both worlds: \* Trade across brc-20, Runes, Taproot Assets, native stablecoins and more in a unified CEX-like trading experience. \* DEX-style self-custody, and permissionless access to all. There is NO compromise on transparency OR performance \* Built for optimal performance: 20x blockspace, 30s confirmation time \* Every transaction committed to blockchain and verifiable as it should be. 🟠 We are coding hard and aim to ship a public testing version by the end of Q2. \*\*\* #### UniScan: The one explorer for all your Bitcoin needs. UniScan provides powerful search across protocols and networks, supporting Ordinals, brc-20, Runes on Bitcoin & Fractal. This supported by our high-performance proprietary brc-20 indexing code, which can process hundreds of blocks per second, with full on-chain data processing taking only a few hours, at least two days to process complete on-chain data. What we shipped in 2025 so far: ✅ Search any brc\*\*-20 tokens, Runes, and Ordinals collections\*\* ✅ \*\*Search any Bitcoin address for instant portfolio visibility:\*\* ✅ Explore full transaction history with advanced filtering options ✅ Clear visualization of transaction details ✅ \*\*Track on-chain activity by protocol\*\*\\ \\ 🟠 Updates:\\ We continue to enhance UniScan with richer features and improved usability: ✅ New independent domain launched: \[uniscan.cc\](https://uniscan.cc) ✅ Protocol support expanded: UniScan now supports Alkanes protocol assets.

![](https://3523236551-files.gitbook.io/~/files/v0/b/gitbook-x-prod.appspot.com/o/spaces%2FJ4NHAHIVnWQiEecvs1By%2Fuploads%2FsCe9fIHuO1OgdBmIzADR%2F4.png?alt=media&token=d0c0172d-84b1-4a44-9626-541304de385c)

![](https://3523236551-files.gitbook.io/~/files/v0/b/gitbook-x-prod.appspot.com/o/spaces%2FJ4NHAHIVnWQiEecvs1By%2Fuploads%2FTPmYyxnunx2WDSxI8FX9%2F6.png?alt=media&token=9c7c7a50-0f9d-4817-b13c-fe062c61e19b)

![](https://3523236551-files.gitbook.io/~/files/v0/b/gitbook-x-prod.appspot.com/o/spaces%2FJ4NHAHIVnWQiEecvs1By%2Fuploads%2FOXB1vOHBFkV0aP1OOwxi%2F5.png?alt=media&token=1de80670-0fb8-4a3b-b8f1-ddf75081a4b0)

\*\*\* ### \*\*Enhancing the Security and Management of Your Assets\*\* #### UniSat Wallet 2.0: Home for Your Bitcoin Assets UniSat Wallet will see critical security upgrades that keep your assets even more secure: \* Providing the option to turn your UniSat Wallet installed device into a cold storage wallet. \* Enabling 2-of-2 multisig wallet with one being your UniSat extension wallet and the other your UniSat App Wallet. 🟠 We’ll be developing in Q2 and aim to ship in Q3. The upgrade will be complete with new features support for everyday utxo management and advanced tools for degen users. What we shipped on Fractal so far: ✅ Merge ready-combined useless (mint and transfer) inscriptions in one click. ✅ Batch select and unlock UTXOs containing various asset types: Ordinals, BRC-20, Runes ✅ Select any UTXOs to lock to avoid unintended spending. ✅ Visualisation of all UTXOs and contained assets ✅ We’ll be launching the UTXO management tool on Bitcoin mainnet in Q2.\\ \\ 🟠 Updates: \\ ✅ UTXO Management Tool has officially launched on the Bitcoin mainnet.

![](https://3523236551-files.gitbook.io/~/files/v0/b/gitbook-x-prod.appspot.com/o/spaces%2FJ4NHAHIVnWQiEecvs1By%2Fuploads%2F8g0dQVjx8Ns9wAmHmu9a%2F7.png?alt=media&token=e21bf7a9-974e-4d2a-9185-2d3d4cfb0b1d)

\*\*\* ### \*\*Decentralized Indexing for an Open Finance System on Bitcoin\*\* Bitcoin has evolved beyond a "money layer" into a comprehensive "data layer" with Ordinals, hosting billions of tokens and their transaction data. However, the ecosystem relies on off-chain indexers—a critical vulnerability. Centralized entities introduce risks, as their mutable state creates potential for manipulation or failure. To ensure long-term growth, a decentralized indexer is essential. UniSat would like to have a go at tackling this challenge. We aim to build a decentralized indexer that can: \* Validate brc-20 and other protocols that Bitcoin nodes can’t natively process. \* Support a broader range of protocols in a secure, incentive-driven network. \* Let users independently verify transactions without relying on centralized entities. 🟠 We’ll commence development work in Q2, and aim to deliver in the second-half of 2025. \*\*\* ### \*\*A Step towards a more global and inclusive community\*\* As UniSat community grows, we now have users from every continent. Even though crypto users are often used to adjusting to English, but we know they’d be much more comfortable using products in their mother tongues. ✅ InSwap was upgraded with multilingual support including French, Spanish, Russian, Japanese, and Traditional Chinese ✅ Multilingual support for UniSat Wallet will be coming to users in Q2. \\ \\ 🟠 Updates: ✅ Multilingual support is now live for both the UniSat Extension Wallet and the UniSat App, including French, Spanish, Russian, Japanese, and Traditional Chinese.

![](https://3523236551-files.gitbook.io/~/files/v0/b/gitbook-x-prod.appspot.com/o/spaces%2FJ4NHAHIVnWQiEecvs1By%2Fuploads%2FoDOgAxI0U2TwY4D75Imm%2F8.png?alt=media&token=9eaae680-11e7-4d9c-8aee-062db7ceb30e)

\*\*\* ### UniSat’s a practical approach to innovation If you’ve made it this far into the article and into the journey with us, we would like to express our heartfelt appreciation, and share a bit more about how we think and how we work. #### \*\*Research-Driven, Decentralized, Open-Minded\*\* As an infrastructure provider, we face a constant influx of new protocols and upgrades requiring support, including our own upgrades! Integrating everything early is easy; choosing wisely and supporting innovations sustainably is harder. We always keep an open mind and never dismiss ideas simply because we don’t yet understand them. Instead, we research thoroughly to answer: \*Does this empower users sustainably? What risks could undermine decentralization or self-custody?\* When in doubt, we pause, learn, and collaborate. Core principles like decentralization and self-custody drive our philosophy. It’s tempting to overlook these when platforms offer sleek user experiences—until the cost of compromising them becomes painfully clear. We aim to support new protocols more efficiently than before, but never at the expense of our foundational values. #### \*\*Execution Over Crafted Narratives\*\* Since the beginning of our journey in 2023, we’ve prioritized action over hype. In an ecosystem dominated by flashy token launches and inflated roadmaps, we stay focused on what matters: \*building\*. Markets fluctuate, but our commitment to transparency and tangible progress remains unwavering. We’ll continue investing time in building—not talking about building—and delivering real benefits for users. #### Multitasking as a trusted service provider For some time since the launch of Fractal mainnet, praises for our work is always accompanied by concerns from the community: are we moving too fast? Are we spreading ourselves too thin? We understand. Our journey began humbly as the first extension wallet to support Ordinals and an inscription platform. The path hasn’t been linear—we grinded when few cared, persevered through setbacks unnoticed, and now we must embrace the responsibility as pioneers. We experiment boldly, push boundaries, and learn from failures, all to improve user experiences and ease the path for future builders. Bitcoin’s ecosystem is still nascent, and its infrastructure gaps demand bold experimentation. We move quickly but never recklessly—every step aims to strengthen Bitcoin’s foundation for the long term. #### Growing with Bitcoin UniSat is maturing into a reliable team. We’re unafraid of short-term failures or doubts because we’re committed to Bitcoin’s future, confident in our team, and inspired by this incredible community. Thank you for your faith in us- and in Bitcoin. Let’s keep building, thoughtfully and relentlessly. UniSat Team

# UniSat 2025 Q2 Work priorities | UniSat

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://docs.unisat.io/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://docs.unisat.io/welcome/unisat-work-priorities/unisat-2025-q2-work-priorities.md)
.

**To the UniSat Community,**

At the start of this year, we shared our [**2025 Work Priorities Overview**](https://docs.unisat.io/home/production-roadmap/overview-of-unisat-2025-work-priorities)
 to keep you informed—and we’re thrilled by how well it’s helped align our efforts with your expectations. Today, we’re excited to provide an update on our progress in 2025 so far and our plans for Q2.

We’ve never stopped building, and we’re hard at work on countless exciting initiatives we can’t wait to unveil.

* * *

Our Focus for Q2 2025[](https://docs.unisat.io/welcome/unisat-work-priorities/unisat-2025-q2-work-priorities#our-focus-for-q2-2025)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

**Elevating Your Bitcoin Trading Experience**

*   InSwap: The first open-sourced brc-20 DEX built natively on Bitcoin and Fractal
    
*   UniHexa: A hybrid trading engine for Bitcoin assets
    

**Protocol Upgrade and Indexing Improvements**

*   brc-20 Single-step transfer
    
*   UniScan: The one explorer for all your Bitcoin needs.
    

**Enhancing the Security and Management of Your Assets**

*   UniSat Wallet 2.0: Home for Your Bitcoin Assets
    

**A Step towards a more global and inclusive community**

*   Multilingual support for InSwap and UniSat Wallet
    

**Decentralized infrastructure for an open finance system on Bitcoin**

*   Decentralized indexing services
    

**UniSat’s practical approach to innovation**

*   Research-Driven, Decentralized, Open-Minded
    
*   Execution Over Crafted Narratives
    
*   Multitasking as a trusted service provider
    

* * *

### Elevating Your Bitcoin Trading Experience[](https://docs.unisat.io/welcome/unisat-work-priorities/unisat-2025-q2-work-priorities#elevating-your-bitcoin-trading-experience)

#### InSwap: the first open-sourced brc-20 DEX[](https://docs.unisat.io/welcome/unisat-work-priorities/unisat-2025-q2-work-priorities#inswap-the-first-open-sourced-brc-20-dex)

Liquidity has been a longstanding issue with brc-20 owing to orderbook marketplace being the most adopted avenue of trading, and having to wait block confirmations on Bitcoin which can take up to 30-40 mins.

InSwap presents an innovative solution as the first b**rc-20** AMM **DEX, allowing users to swap, provide liquidity and earn brc-20 easily on Bitcoin and Fractal.**

The automated market maker (AMM) model enables brc-20 trades to always be executed with an algorithmically determined price, without needing a counterparty.

It is powered by a decentralized swap module that aggregates transactions in batches using a novel sequencer which enables:

*   Instant swaps without waiting for block confirmation
    
*   Large volumes to be processed simultaneously
    
*   Robust decentralization and security
    

InSwap mainnet has been live since September 2024, we shipped several key updates in 2025 so far:

✅ Integrated wrap: easier and faster wrap into swap module

✅ New domain and multilingual support: [https://inswap.cc/](https://inswap.cc/)
 and languages like traditional chinese

✅ UI cleaning: Charts and pool info

✅ Runes swap (wrapped as a token brc-20) has been successfully implemented using SUSD and we plan to roll out more assets later in Q2.

🎡 LPfest: provide liquidity for trading pairs and get a share service fee and additional rewards in LPfest.

![](https://docs.unisat.io/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3523236551-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FJ4NHAHIVnWQiEecvs1By%252Fuploads%252FHDJJcTsoKM3Z67DKhoGu%252FScreenshot%25202025-04-29%2520at%252017.07.09%2520%281%29.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D9678b9c2-5a00-4954-88ae-dd1694c00fe1&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=95b97043d275ff2ddd5f4d7c9cc53c6e&sv=3)

What we planned for Q2:

✅ Regular LPfest epochs to boost liquidity rewards

✅ Better visualization of activities in InSwap and search feature

✅ More experimentation on how to improve trade routing and user onboarding

🟠 Updates：

*   Hosted multiple LPfest Epoch events: Epoch 3, 3.1, 4, and 4.1 successfully completed.
    
*   Run several InSwap trading events with community partners.
    
*   Feature upgrades:
    
    *   Added LP Burn feature
        
    *   Introduced One-click sign for swaps
        
    *   Enabled Custom fee rates for wrap operations
        
    *   Added Bulk Send for brc-20 transfers to multiple addresses

#### brc-20 Swap module on Bitcoin[](https://docs.unisat.io/welcome/unisat-work-priorities/unisat-2025-q2-work-priorities#brc-20-swap-module-on-bitcoin)

InSwap will be deployed on Bitcoin mainnet pending implementation of the brc-20 swap module on Bitcoin, which requires brc-20 protocol upgrade and unilateral approval from all relevant parties. Therefore, we chose to deploy on Fractal first.

✅ In Q1, we included two additional implementations of the swap module on Fractal to ensure maximum reliability.

Once brc-20 upgrade is approved, we will aim to launch InSwap on Bitcoin testnet by Q2 2025.

This means that you will have the option to either roll up your transactions onto Bitcoin mainnet when trading assets on Bitcoin, or roll up transactions onto Fractal for lower roll up fees. 🟠 Updates: We are currently waiting for the brc-20 upgrade to be approved and will continue to keep the community updated as progress unfolds.

* * *

#### **brc-20: Single-step transfer**[](https://docs.unisat.io/welcome/unisat-work-priorities/unisat-2025-q2-work-priorities#brc-20-single-step-transfer)

In the plainest sense, single-step transfer means you no longer need to inscribe a ‘transfer’ inscription and wait for block confirmations before sending brc-20!

Our progress in 2025 so far:

✅ UniSat developed the solution to combine brc-20 transfer into a single step. We have deployed this upgrade on Fractal testnet and within UniSat inscribe and UniSat Wallet.

✅ We are slightly behind the announced schedule as this requires us to work closely with other indexers. The good news is we have completed development of the indexer implementations for the other main brc-20 indexers OKX and OPI, and we’re working with them to integrate.

What we planned for Q2:

✅ Once sufficiently tested, **Single-step transfer** will be deployed on Fractal mainnet by Q2 2025.

✅ We will work with other indexers and brc-20 maintainers to push for its implementation on Bitcoin mainnet.

If you enjoyed the experience of InSwap and single-step transfer and would like to see it on Bitcoin mainnet. Please let us hear your voice and support us in pushing this forward!

![](https://docs.unisat.io/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3523236551-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FJ4NHAHIVnWQiEecvs1By%252Fuploads%252FRFAQpdEXPLOozuZu7Vif%252F3.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dceab332d-600f-46d1-b740-1212d1800f44&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=28965c37e358cc2153f428224e66babf&sv=3)

🟠 Updates: ✅ brc-20 Single-Step Transfer is now live on Fractal Mainnet and we will closely monitor its performance on Fractal network and continue to advocate for its deployment on Bitcoin mainnet.

#### UniHexa: the next-gen hybrid trading engine for Bitcoin assets[](https://docs.unisat.io/welcome/unisat-work-priorities/unisat-2025-q2-work-priorities#unihexa-the-next-gen-hybrid-trading-engine-for-bitcoin-assets)

UniHexa brings you the best of both worlds:

*   Trade across brc-20, Runes, Taproot Assets, native stablecoins and more in a unified CEX-like trading experience.
    
*   DEX-style self-custody, and permissionless access to all.
    

There is NO compromise on transparency OR performance

*   Built for optimal performance: 20x blockspace, 30s confirmation time
    
*   Every transaction committed to blockchain and verifiable as it should be.
    

🟠 We are coding hard and aim to ship a public testing version by the end of Q2.

#### UniScan: The one explorer for all your Bitcoin needs.[](https://docs.unisat.io/welcome/unisat-work-priorities/unisat-2025-q2-work-priorities#uniscan-the-one-explorer-for-all-your-bitcoin-needs)

UniScan provides powerful search across protocols and networks, supporting Ordinals, brc-20, Runes on Bitcoin & Fractal.

This supported by our high-performance proprietary brc-20 indexing code, which can process hundreds of blocks per second, with full on-chain data processing taking only a few hours, at least two days to process complete on-chain data.

What we shipped in 2025 so far:

✅ Search any brc**\-20 tokens, Runes, and Ordinals collections**

✅ **Search any Bitcoin address for instant portfolio visibility:**

✅ Explore full transaction history with advanced filtering options

✅ Clear visualization of transaction details

✅ **Track on-chain activity by protocol** 🟠 Updates: We continue to enhance UniScan with richer features and improved usability:

✅ New independent domain launched: [uniscan.cc](https://uniscan.cc/)

✅ Protocol support expanded: UniScan now supports Alkanes protocol assets.

### **Enhancing the Security and Management of Your Assets**[](https://docs.unisat.io/welcome/unisat-work-priorities/unisat-2025-q2-work-priorities#enhancing-the-security-and-management-of-your-assets)

#### UniSat Wallet 2.0: Home for Your Bitcoin Assets[](https://docs.unisat.io/welcome/unisat-work-priorities/unisat-2025-q2-work-priorities#unisat-wallet-2.0-home-for-your-bitcoin-assets)

UniSat Wallet will see critical security upgrades that keep your assets even more secure:

*   Providing the option to turn your UniSat Wallet installed device into a cold storage wallet.
    
*   Enabling 2-of-2 multisig wallet with one being your UniSat extension wallet and the other your UniSat App Wallet.
    

🟠 We’ll be developing in Q2 and aim to ship in Q3.

The upgrade will be complete with new features support for everyday utxo management and advanced tools for degen users.

What we shipped on Fractal so far:

✅ Merge ready-combined useless (mint and transfer) inscriptions in one click.

✅ Batch select and unlock UTXOs containing various asset types: Ordinals, BRC-20, Runes

✅ Select any UTXOs to lock to avoid unintended spending.

✅ Visualisation of all UTXOs and contained assets

✅ We’ll be launching the UTXO management tool on Bitcoin mainnet in Q2. 🟠 Updates: ✅ UTXO Management Tool has officially launched on the Bitcoin mainnet.

### **Decentralized Indexing for an Open Finance System on Bitcoin**[](https://docs.unisat.io/welcome/unisat-work-priorities/unisat-2025-q2-work-priorities#decentralized-indexing-for-an-open-finance-system-on-bitcoin)

Bitcoin has evolved beyond a "money layer" into a comprehensive "data layer" with Ordinals, hosting billions of tokens and their transaction data. However, the ecosystem relies on off-chain indexers—a critical vulnerability. Centralized entities introduce risks, as their mutable state creates potential for manipulation or failure. To ensure long-term growth, a decentralized indexer is essential.

UniSat would like to have a go at tackling this challenge. We aim to build a decentralized indexer that can:

*   Validate brc-20 and other protocols that Bitcoin nodes can’t natively process.
    
*   Support a broader range of protocols in a secure, incentive-driven network.
    
*   Let users independently verify transactions without relying on centralized entities.
    

🟠 We’ll commence development work in Q2, and aim to deliver in the second-half of 2025.

### UniSat’s a practical approach to innovation[](https://docs.unisat.io/welcome/unisat-work-priorities/unisat-2025-q2-work-priorities#unisats-a-practical-approach-to-innovation)

If you’ve made it this far into the article and into the journey with us, we would like to express our heartfelt appreciation, and share a bit more about how we think and how we work.

#### **Research-Driven, Decentralized, Open-Minded**[](https://docs.unisat.io/welcome/unisat-work-priorities/unisat-2025-q2-work-priorities#research-driven-decentralized-open-minded)

As an infrastructure provider, we face a constant influx of new protocols and upgrades requiring support, including our own upgrades! Integrating everything early is easy; choosing wisely and supporting innovations sustainably is harder.

We always keep an open mind and never dismiss ideas simply because we don’t yet understand them. Instead, we research thoroughly to answer: _Does this empower users sustainably? What risks could undermine decentralization or self-custody?_ When in doubt, we pause, learn, and collaborate.

Core principles like decentralization and self-custody drive our philosophy. It’s tempting to overlook these when platforms offer sleek user experiences—until the cost of compromising them becomes painfully clear. We aim to support new protocols more efficiently than before, but never at the expense of our foundational values.

#### **Execution Over Crafted Narratives**[](https://docs.unisat.io/welcome/unisat-work-priorities/unisat-2025-q2-work-priorities#execution-over-crafted-narratives)

Since the beginning of our journey in 2023, we’ve prioritized action over hype. In an ecosystem dominated by flashy token launches and inflated roadmaps, we stay focused on what matters: _building_. Markets fluctuate, but our commitment to transparency and tangible progress remains unwavering.

We’ll continue investing time in building—not talking about building—and delivering real benefits for users.

#### Multitasking as a trusted service provider[](https://docs.unisat.io/welcome/unisat-work-priorities/unisat-2025-q2-work-priorities#multitasking-as-a-trusted-service-provider)

For some time since the launch of Fractal mainnet, praises for our work is always accompanied by concerns from the community: are we moving too fast? Are we spreading ourselves too thin?

We understand. Our journey began humbly as the first extension wallet to support Ordinals and an inscription platform. The path hasn’t been linear—we grinded when few cared, persevered through setbacks unnoticed, and now we must embrace the responsibility as pioneers. We experiment boldly, push boundaries, and learn from failures, all to improve user experiences and ease the path for future builders.

Bitcoin’s ecosystem is still nascent, and its infrastructure gaps demand bold experimentation. We move quickly but never recklessly—every step aims to strengthen Bitcoin’s foundation for the long term.

#### Growing with Bitcoin[](https://docs.unisat.io/welcome/unisat-work-priorities/unisat-2025-q2-work-priorities#growing-with-bitcoin)

UniSat is maturing into a reliable team. We’re unafraid of short-term failures or doubts because we’re committed to Bitcoin’s future, confident in our team, and inspired by this incredible community.

Thank you for your faith in us- and in Bitcoin. Let’s keep building, thoughtfully and relentlessly.

UniSat Team

Last updated 9 months ago

# UniSat Work Priorities | UniSat

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://docs.unisat.io/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://docs.unisat.io/welcome/unisat-work-priorities.md)
.

Welcome to UniSat’s Work Priorities page — a transparent, living overview of the core initiatives and focus development cycle.

This section outlines what we’re actively building, improving, or exploring. It reflects our evolving priorities as we grow the UniSat ecosystem, while staying responsive to community feedback and protocol-level changes.

What you'll find here: 🌱 Our goals

🛠️ Feature and product development priorities

📊 Infrastructure, ecosystem, and scalability work

We believe in building in the open — this page is part of that commitment.

Note: Items listed here are not guaranteed release targets or deadlines. Priorities may shift based on technical discoveries, user demand, or ecosystem needs.

For the latest product updates or changelogs, please refer to our Release Notes or Dev Updates on X.

UniSat Website: [https://unisat.io/](https://unisat.io/)

X: [https://x.com/unisat\_wallet](https://x.com/unisat_wallet)

Discord: [http://discord.gg/unisat](http://discord.gg/unisat)

Last updated 8 months ago

# Production Roadmap in Q4 2024 | UniSat

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://docs.unisat.io/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://docs.unisat.io/welcome/unisat-work-priorities/production-roadmap-in-q4-2024.md)
.

As we enter the final quarter of 2024, our focus remains on delivering and optimizing our products to provide top-tier infrastructure support for both Bitcoin and Fractal. We are excited to share our upcoming roadmap to encourage proactive and meaningful engagement with the community as we continue our journey together.

_(please note this is a draft version that will be revised periodically as we progress)_

Roadmap in Q4 2024[](https://docs.unisat.io/welcome/unisat-work-priorities/production-roadmap-in-q4-2024#roadmap-in-q4-2024)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### October 2024[](https://docs.unisat.io/welcome/unisat-work-priorities/production-roadmap-in-q4-2024#october-2024)

*   **Runes activation on Fractal**
    
    *   (Engineering complexity: ⭐️⭐️⭐️; Technical innovation: ⭐️⭐️; Scheduling certainty: ⭐️⭐️⭐️⭐️⭐️)
        
    *   Implement Runes on Fractal (lowercase)
        
    *   Post-activation: Enable SimpleBridge to bridge Bitcoin mainnet Runes onto Fractal, so that they can interact with Runes on Fractal directly
        
    
*   **CAT20 identifying and transferring**
    
    *   (Engineering complexity: ⭐️⭐️⭐️; Technical innovation: ⭐️⭐️⭐️⭐️; Scheduling certainty: ⭐️⭐️⭐️⭐️)
        
    *   UniSat Wallet: Enable CAT20 recognition, display and transfers
        
    *   UniSat Explorer: Identify CAT20 assets and transactions
        
    
*   **CAT Market (trustless CAT20 trading)**
    
    *   (Engineering complexity: ⭐️⭐️⭐️⭐️⭐️; Technical innovation: ⭐️⭐️⭐️⭐️⭐️; Scheduling certainty: ⭐️⭐️⭐️)
        
    *   Implement a trustless, permissionless Bitcoin script-based on-chain CAT20 trading platform
        
    *   Support for both buy orders and sell orders (more flexible than PSBT), but partial order fulfillment is not supported yet due to script limitations (without OP\_MUL)
        
    

### November 2024[](https://docs.unisat.io/welcome/unisat-work-priorities/production-roadmap-in-q4-2024#november-2024)

*   **Fractal on-chain voting**
    
    *   (Engineering complexity: ⭐️⭐️⭐️⭐️; Technical innovation: ⭐️⭐️⭐️⭐️⭐️; Scheduling certainty: ⭐️⭐️⭐️⭐️)
        
    *   Design and implement OP\_CAT-based locking and voting model
        
    *   Apply transparent proposal submission, discussion and adoption for Fractal
        
    
*   **InSwap major update**
    
    *   (Engineering complexity: ⭐️⭐️⭐️; Technical innovation: ⭐️⭐️⭐️⭐️; Scheduling certainty: ⭐️⭐️⭐️⭐️⭐️)
        
    *   UniSat Points and FB Rewards for specific Liquidity Providers
        
    *   Simplified operating procedures for InSwap
        
    *   Opening up to all tickers on Fractal.
        
    
*   **brc-20, Runes & CAT20 optimization**
    
    *   (Engineering complexity: ⭐️⭐️⭐️⭐️⭐️; Technical innovation: ⭐️⭐️; Scheduling certainty: ⭐️⭐️⭐️)
        
    *   Enhance UniSat indexing infrastructure to achieve faster block parsing with all supporting protocols (target response time: 15-30s)
        
    *   Optimize the responsiveness (balance updating and UTXO retrieving) of UniSat Wallet (target response time: 15-30s)
        
    

### December 2024[](https://docs.unisat.io/welcome/unisat-work-priorities/production-roadmap-in-q4-2024#december-2024)

*   **zkAtomicSwap - effective atomic swapping**
    
    *   (Engineering complexity: ⭐️⭐️⭐️⭐️⭐️; Technical innovation: ⭐️⭐️⭐️⭐️⭐️; Scheduling certainty: ⭐️⭐️⭐️)
        
    *   Implement atomic swap between different chains & different protocols
        
    *   Trustless trading mechanism between Fractal and other ecosystem
        
    
*   **CAT Protocol advanced support**
    
    *   (Engineering complexity: ⭐️⭐️⭐️⭐️; Technical innovation: ⭐️⭐️⭐️⭐️; Scheduling certainty: ⭐️⭐️⭐️)
        
    *   Advanced support for cat721 and cat-dApps across all UniSat products
        
    *   Further consolidating product-side implementation of the CAT protocol
        
    
*   **InSwap major update**
    
    *   (Engineering complexity: ⭐️⭐️⭐️; Technical innovation: ⭐️⭐️⭐️⭐️; Scheduling certainty: ⭐️⭐️⭐️⭐️)
        
    *   Perform the upgrade of the BRC-20 swap module on Bitcoin mainnet.
        
    *   Build unified and consistent accessing model for both Bitcoin and Fractal for InSwap
        
    

UniSat Team
