.

Copy

On this page

Get the orders associated with your API-KEY

get

https://open-api.unisat.ioMainnet endpoint for public

/v2/inscribe/order/list

Get order list of current apikey

Authorizations

bearerAuth

AuthorizationstringRequired

Bearer authentication header of the form Bearer <token>.

Query parameters

cursorintegerRequired

Start offset

Example: `0`

sizeintegerRequired

Number of items returned

Example: `16`

sortstring · enumOptional

Sort by (asc/desc)

Example: `asc`Possible values: `asc``desc`

statusstring · enumOptional

Status of order

Possible values: `pending``inscribing``minted``closed``refunded`

receiveAddressstringOptional

ReceiveAddress of order

clientIdstringOptional

ClientId of order

Responses

200

Successful operation

application/json

codeinteger · enumOptionalPossible values: `0``-1`

msgstringOptionalExample: `OK`

dataobjectOptional

Show properties

401

Invalid API Key

get/v2/inscribe/order/list

HTTP

Copy

    GET /v2/inscribe/order/list?cursor=1&size=1 HTTP/1.1
    Host: open-api.unisat.io
    Authorization: Bearer YOUR_SECRET_TOKEN
    Accept: */*
    

Test it

200

Successful operation

Copy

    {
      "code": 0,
      "msg": "OK",
      "data": {
        "detail": [\
          {\
            "orderId": "",\
            "status": "pending",\
            "payAddress": "",\
            "receiveAddress": "",\
            "amount": 3000,\
            "paidAmount": 1,\
            "outputValue": 1,\
            "feeRate": 1,\
            "minerFee": 1,\
            "serviceFee": 1,\
            "devFee": 1,\
            "files": [\
              {\
                "filename": "10000.sats",\
                "inscriptionId": "",\
                "status": "pending"\
              }\
            ],\
            "count": 1,\
            "pendingCount": 1,\
            "unconfirmedCount": 0,\
            "confirmedCount": 0,\
            "createTime": 1693439128100,\
            "refundTxid": "",\
            "refundAmount": 1,\
            "refundFeeRate": 1\
          }\
        ],
        "start": 1,
        "total": 1
      }
    }

*   status (Optional) : pending/inscribing/minted/closed/refunded
    
*   receiveAddress (Optional): Filter by receive address
    
*   clientId (Optional): The clientId passed when creating an order is useful for filtering a certain user's orders.
    

![](https://docs.unisat.io/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3523236551-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FJ4NHAHIVnWQiEecvs1By%252Fuploads%252FT8RhSfWXSXR3We3kdSZ3%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dc1e67a13-c71d-49fd-aea9-96dcaaca757c&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=eaa48f26dfde8a76e89d096831ed2e34&sv=3)

On this page

### Get runes status[](https://docs.unisat.io/developer-support/open-api-documentation/api-for-fractal-bitcoin/runes-on-fractal/get-runes-status#get-v1-indexer-runes-status)

get

https://open-api-fractal.unisat.ioMainnet endpoint for public.

/v1/indexer/runes/status

Get runes global status

Authorizations

bearerAuth

AuthorizationstringRequired

Bearer authentication header of the form Bearer <token>.

Responses

200

successful operation

application/json

codeintegerOptionalExample: `0`

msgstringOptional

dataobject · RuneStatusOptional

Show properties

get/v1/indexer/runes/status

HTTP

Copy

    Host: open-api-fractal.unisat.io
    Authorization: Bearer YOUR_SECRET_TOKEN
    Accept: */*

successful operation

    {

      "msg": "",
      "data": {
        "bestHeight": 1,
        "runes": 100,
        "minimumRune": "AAAAAAAAAAAA",
        "halvingBlockCount": 100
      }
    }

*   `bestHeight` : The latest height of the data being processed by the current index
    
*   `runes`: The total count of Runes
    
*   `minimumRune`: The minimumRune can be edicted
    
*   `halvingBlockCount`: The number of blocks remaining until the next halving

# Get Token Price | UniSat

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://docs.unisat.io/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://docs.unisat.io/developer-support/open-api-documentation/api-for-fractal-bitcoin/cat20-dex/get-token-price.md)
.

Copy

On this page

### Get token price[](https://docs.unisat.io/developer-support/open-api-documentation/api-for-fractal-bitcoin/cat20-dex/get-token-price#get-v1-cat20-dex-gettokenprice)

get

https://open-api-fractal.unisat.ioMainnet endpoint for public.

/v1/cat20-dex/getTokenPrice

Authorizations

bearerAuth

AuthorizationstringRequired

Bearer authentication header of the form Bearer <token>.

Query parameters

tokenIdstringRequired

tokenId

Example: `45ee725c2c5993b3e4d308842d87e973bf1951f5f7a804b21e4dd964ecd12d6b_0`

Responses

200

Successful operation

application/json

codeinteger · int32Optional

msgstringOptionalExample: `OK`

dataobjectOptional

Show properties

401

Invalid API Key

get/v1/cat20-dex/getTokenPrice

HTTP

Copy

    GET /v1/cat20-dex/getTokenPrice?tokenId=text HTTP/1.1
    Host: open-api-fractal.unisat.io
    Authorization: Bearer YOUR_SECRET_TOKEN
    Accept: */*
    

Test it

200

Successful operation

Copy

    {
      "code": 1,
      "msg": "OK",
      "data": {
        "askPrice": 1,
        "bidPrice": 1,
        "latestTradePrice": 1,
        "timestamp": "text",
        "height": 1
      }
    }

*   Please note that the values here do not include decimal places.
    
    *   `"askPrice": 197000` refers to 197000 satoshis / 0.01 CAT, which equates to 0.197 FB/CAT after conversion.
        
    
*   `askPrice` represents the current lowest selling price from sellers.
    
*   `bidPrice` represents the current highest bid from buyers.
    

![](https://docs.unisat.io/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3523236551-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FJ4NHAHIVnWQiEecvs1By%252Fuploads%252FQDWHkbJNXUxZxrHUOjqu%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D34edeed7-db94-4b7b-b120-2346955806f3&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=91a2a196cb330c27e5763a98feaafe2f&sv=3)

# Get Market Stats | UniSat

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://docs.unisat.io/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://docs.unisat.io/developer-support/open-api-documentation/api-for-fractal-bitcoin/cat20-dex/get-market-stats.md)
.

Copy

On this page

### Get the market stats[](https://docs.unisat.io/developer-support/open-api-documentation/api-for-fractal-bitcoin/cat20-dex/get-market-stats#get-v1-cat20-dex-getmarketstats)

get

https://open-api-fractal.unisat.ioMainnet endpoint for public.

/v1/cat20-dex/getMarketStats

Retrieves the market stats for all tokens which have trades in the last 30 days

Authorizations

bearerAuth

AuthorizationstringRequired

Bearer authentication header of the form Bearer <token>.

Query parameters

sortFieldenum · enumOptional

The field to sort by ('volume', 'volume30d', 'volume7d', 'volume24h', 'volume6h')

Possible values: `volume``volume30d``volume7d``volume24h``volume6h`

tokenIdstringOptional

tokenId

Example: `45ee725c2c5993b3e4d308842d87e973bf1951f5f7a804b21e4dd964ecd12d6b_0`

offsetintegerOptional

The offset to start from

Example: `0`

limitintegerOptional

The limit of the stats (default 20, max 100)

Example: `20`

Responses

200

Successful operation

application/json

codeinteger · int32Optional

msgstringOptionalExample: `OK`

dataobjectOptional

Show properties

401

Invalid API Key

get/v1/cat20-dex/getMarketStats

HTTP

Copy

    GET /v1/cat20-dex/getMarketStats HTTP/1.1
    Host: open-api-fractal.unisat.io
    Authorization: Bearer YOUR_SECRET_TOKEN
    Accept: */*
    

Test it

200

Successful operation

Copy

    {
      "code": 1,
      "msg": "OK",
      "data": {
        "tokenStats": {
          "tokenId": "text",
          "volume": "text",
          "volume30d": "text",
          "volume24h": "text",
          "volume7d": "text",
          "volume6h": "text",
          "price": 1,
          "price6h": 1,
          "price24h": 1,
          "price7d": 1,
          "price30d": 1,
          "name": "text",
          "symbol": "text",
          "decimals": 1,
          "max": 1,
          "volumeToken": "text",
          "volumeToken30d": "text",
          "volumeToken24h": "text",
          "volumeToken7d": "text",
          "volumeToken6h": "text"
        },
        "total": 1
      }
    }

*   Please note that the values here do not include decimal places.
    
    *   `"price": 198000` refers to 198000 satoshis / 0.01 CAT, which equates to 0.198 FB/CAT after conversion.
        
    
*   The unit for volume is satoshis.
    
    *   `"volume": "7169599786237"` translates to a total transaction volume of 71695.99786237 FB.

![](https://docs.unisat.io/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3523236551-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FJ4NHAHIVnWQiEecvs1By%252Fuploads%252FNgzmUYeqNlFVj9AHCxp7%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D9325e904-688d-46c9-a65c-04bc05dd4f24&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=5373d701dfb5b93358abdc40152b3cc3&sv=3)

Due to the inability to transmit special characters on the router, for tickers containing special characters, please use the hexadecimal encoding format.

    export function stringToHex(stringToEncode: string) {
        return Buffer.from(stringToEncode).toString('hex')

https://open-api.unisat.ioMainnet endpoint for public

/v1/indexer/runes/status

Get runes global status

Authorizations

bearerAuth

AuthorizationstringRequired

Bearer authentication header of the form Bearer <token>.

Responses

200

successful operation

application/json

codeintegerOptionalExample: `0`

msgstringOptional

dataobject · RuneStatusOptional

Show properties

get/v1/indexer/runes/status

HTTP

    Host: open-api.unisat.io

successful operation

Copy

    {

        "runes": 100,

      }

*   `minimumRune`: The minimumRune can be edicted
    
*   `halvingBlockCount`: The number of blocks remaining until the next halving

get

https://open-api.unisat.ioMainnet endpoint for public

/v1/indexer/address/{address}/brc20/summary-by-height/{height}

Obtain BRC20 token summary by address, including available balance, transferable balance

Authorizations

bearerAuth

AuthorizationstringRequired

Bearer authentication header of the form Bearer <token>.

Path parameters

addressstringRequired

Address

heightstringRequired

Block Height

Query parameters

startintegerRequired

Start offset

Example: `0`

limitintegerRequired

Number of inscriptions returned

Example: `16`

Responses

200

Successful operation

application/json

codeinteger · enumOptionalPossible values: `0``-1`

msgstringOptionalExample: `OK`

dataobjectOptional

Show properties

401

Invalid API Key

get/v1/indexer/address/{address}/brc20/summary-by-height/{height}

HTTP

Copy

    GET /v1/indexer/address/{address}/brc20/summary-by-height/{height}?start=1&limit=1 HTTP/1.1
    Host: open-api.unisat.io
    Authorization: Bearer YOUR_SECRET_TOKEN
    Accept: */*
    

Test it

200

Successful operation

Copy

    {
      "code": 0,
      "msg": "OK",
      "data": {
        "height": 1,
        "start": 1,
        "total": 1,
        "detail": [\
          {\
            "ticker": "text",\
            "overallBalance": "text",\
            "transferableBalance": "text",\
            "availableBalance": "text"\
          }\
        ]
      }
    }

Each ticker includes two types of balances:

*   transferableBalance: The balance already inscribed as TRANSFER inscriptions
    
*   availableBalance: The balance can be inscribed as TRANSFER inscriptions
    
*   overallBalance = transferableBalance+availableBalance

.

**Fractal Testnet Developer Notices**

*   **Wallet Integration**: When integrating the UniSat wallet, please ensure you are using the **FRACTAL\_BITCOIN\_TESTNET** network.
    
*   **Open-API Endpoint**: The Open-API endpoint has been updated. Developers should now use the following endpoint for all API requests: [https://open-api-fractal-testnet.unisat.io](https://open-api-fractal-testnet.unisat.io/)
    
*   Please apply for new API Key on our developer center [https://unisat.io/plans](https://unisat.io/plans)
    

These updates are essential for ensuring compatibility and optimal performance during the test phase. We encourage all developers to make the necessary adjustments to their integrations.

        "unconfirmedCount": 0,
        "confirmedCount": 0,

FAQ[](https://docs.unisat.io/developer-support/open-api-documentation/api-for-bitcoin/unisat-inscribe/create-order#faq)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### How to pay after create an order?[](https://docs.unisat.io/developer-support/open-api-documentation/api-for-bitcoin/unisat-inscribe/create-order#how-to-pay-after-create-an-order)

The order will start inscribing once the "payAddress" receives the "amount". For example, if an order requires 3000 satoshis, you have to send 3000 satoshis to payAddress. After that, the inscription will be inscribed.

### How is the open-api order charged?[](https://docs.unisat.io/developer-support/open-api-documentation/api-for-bitcoin/unisat-inscribe/create-order#how-is-the-open-api-order-charged)

We charge a base service fee of 1999 for the first 25 inscriptions, along with an additional network service fee of 4.99%. We round down the final cost.

### Are the orders on the open-api and the UniSat website interoperable?[](https://docs.unisat.io/developer-support/open-api-documentation/api-for-bitcoin/unisat-inscribe/create-order#are-the-orders-on-the-open-api-and-the-unisat-website-interoperable)

No.

Open API and UniSat website inscribing service do not share data. This ensures the stability of the Open API interface and prevents it from being affected by frequent changes to website inscribing service. For similar reasons, the orders on open-api do not enjoy discounts from the OG card or .unisat domain.

### How many files can I submit for each order?[](https://docs.unisat.io/developer-support/open-api-documentation/api-for-bitcoin/unisat-inscribe/create-order#how-many-files-can-i-submit-for-each-order)

An order supports a maximum of 2000 files, with each file having a maximum size of 390 KB

### How to estimate fee before create an order?[](https://docs.unisat.io/developer-support/open-api-documentation/api-for-bitcoin/unisat-inscribe/create-order#how-to-estimate-fee-before-create-an-order)

Last updated 2 years ago

*   [postCreate an order](https://docs.unisat.io/developer-support/open-api-documentation/api-for-bitcoin/unisat-inscribe/create-order#post-v2-inscribe-order-create)
    
*   [FAQ](https://docs.unisat.io/developer-support/open-api-documentation/api-for-bitcoin/unisat-inscribe/create-order#faq)
    
*   [How to pay after create an order?](https://docs.unisat.io/developer-support/open-api-documentation/api-for-bitcoin/unisat-inscribe/create-order#how-to-pay-after-create-an-order)
    
*   [How is the open-api order charged?](https://docs.unisat.io/developer-support/open-api-documentation/api-for-bitcoin/unisat-inscribe/create-order#how-is-the-open-api-order-charged)
    
*   [Are the orders on the open-api and the UniSat website interoperable?](https://docs.unisat.io/developer-support/open-api-documentation/api-for-bitcoin/unisat-inscribe/create-order#are-the-orders-on-the-open-api-and-the-unisat-website-interoperable)
    
*   [How many files can I submit for each order?](https://docs.unisat.io/developer-support/open-api-documentation/api-for-bitcoin/unisat-inscribe/create-order#how-many-files-can-i-submit-for-each-order)
    
*   [How to estimate fee before create an order?](https://docs.unisat.io/developer-support/open-api-documentation/api-for-bitcoin/unisat-inscribe/create-order#how-to-estimate-fee-before-create-an-order)
    

Copy

    const address = ''; // the receiver address
    const inscriptionBalance = 546; // the balance in each inscription
    const fileCount = 1000; // the fileCount
    const fileSize = 1000; // the total size of all files
    const contentTypeSize = 100; // the size of contentType
    const feeRate = 10; // the feeRate
    const feeFileSize = 100; // the total size of first 25 files
    const feeFileCount = 25 // do not change this
    const devFee = 1000; // the fee for developer
    
    const balance = inscriptionBalance * fileCount;
    
    let addrSize = 25+1; // p2pkh
    if(address.indexOf('bc1q')==0 || address.indexOf('tb1q')==0){
        addrSize = 22+1;
    }else if(address.indexOf('bc1p')==0 || address.indexOf('tb1p')==0){
        addrSize = 34+1;
    }else if(address.indexOf('2')==0 || address.indexOf('3')==0){
        addrSize = 23+1;
    }
    
    const baseSize = 88;
    let networkSats = Math.ceil(((fileSize+contentTypeSize) / 4 + ( baseSize+8+addrSize+8+23)) * feeRate);
    if(fileCount>1){
        networkSats = Math.ceil(((fileSize+contentTypeSize) / 4 + (baseSize+8+addrSize+(35+8)*(fileCount-1)+ 8+23 +(baseSize+8+addrSize+0.5)*(fileCount-1) )) * feeRate);
    }
    let networkSatsByFeeCount = Math.ceil(((feeFileSize+contentTypeSize) / 4 + ( baseSize+8+addrSize+8+23)) * feeRate);
    if(fileCount>1){
        networkSatsByFeeCount = Math.ceil(((( feeFileSize)+contentTypeSize*(feeFileCount/fileCount)) / 4 + (baseSize+8+addrSize+(35+8)*(fileCount-1)+ 8+23 +(baseSize+8+addrSize+0.5)*Math.min(fileCount-1,feeFileCount-1) )) * feeRate);
    }
    
    const baseFee = 1999 * Math.min(fileCount, feeFileCount); // 1999 base fee for top 25 files
    const floatFee = Math.ceil(networkSatsByFeeCount * 0.0499); // 4.99% extra miner fee for top 25 transations
    const serviceFee = Math.floor(baseFee + floatFee);
    
    const total = balance + networkSats + serviceFee; 
    const truncatedTotal = Math.floor((total) / 1000) * 1000; // truncate
    const amount = truncatedTotal + devFee; // add devFee at the end
    
    console.log("The final amount need to pay: ",amount)

---

Copy

    
    enum OrderStatus {
        // when create order
        pending = 'pending',
        
        // pay not enough, need pay more
        payment_notenough = 'payment_notenough',
        
        // pay over, need choose continue or refund
        payment_overpay = 'payment_overpay',
        
        // there is an inscription in payment transaction, need refund
        payment_withinscription = 'payment_withinscription',
        
        // in some case, payment transaction need be confirmed
        payment_waitconfirmed = 'payment_waitconfirmed',
        
        // payment success
        payment_success = 'payment_success',
        
        // ready to inscribe
        ready = 'ready',
        inscribing = 'inscribing',
        minted = 'minted',
        closed = 'closed',
        refunded = 'refunded',
        cancel = 'cancel',
    }
    
    
    enum InscriptionStatus {
      pending = 'pending',
      unconfirmed = 'unconfirmed',
      confirmed = 'confirmed'
    }

On this page

get

https://open-api-fractal.unisat.ioMainnet endpoint for public.

/v1/indexer/address/{address}/brc20/summary

Obtain BRC20 token summary by address, including available balance, transferable balance

Authorizations

bearerAuth

AuthorizationstringRequired

Bearer authentication header of the form Bearer <token>.

Path parameters

addressstringRequired

Address

Query parameters

startintegerRequired

Start offset

Example: `0`

limitintegerRequired

Number of inscriptions returned

Example: `16`

Responses

200

Successful operation

application/json

codeinteger · enumOptionalPossible values: `0``-1`

msgstringOptionalExample: `OK`

dataobjectOptional

Show properties

401

Invalid API Key

get/v1/indexer/address/{address}/brc20/summary

HTTP

Copy

    GET /v1/indexer/address/{address}/brc20/summary?start=1&limit=1 HTTP/1.1
    Host: open-api-fractal.unisat.io
    Authorization: Bearer YOUR_SECRET_TOKEN
    Accept: */*
    

Test it

200

Successful operation

Copy

    {
      "code": 0,
      "msg": "OK",
      "data": {
        "height": 1,
        "start": 1,
        "total": 1,
        "detail": [\
          {\
            "ticker": "text",\
            "overallBalance": "text",\
            "transferableBalance": "text",\
            "availableBalance": "text"\
          }\
        ]
      }
    }

Each ticker includes two types of balances:

*   transferableBalance: The balance already inscribed as TRANSFER inscriptions
    
*   availableBalance: The balance can be inscribed as TRANSFER inscriptions
    
*   overallBalance = transferableBalance+availableBalance

# Refund estimate | UniSat

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://docs.unisat.io/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://docs.unisat.io/developer-support/open-api-documentation/api-for-bitcoin/unisat-inscribe/refund-estimate.md)
.

Copy

On this page

### Estimate the size of the refund transaction[](https://docs.unisat.io/developer-support/open-api-documentation/api-for-bitcoin/unisat-inscribe/refund-estimate#post-v2-inscribe-order-orderid-refund-estimate)

post

https://open-api.unisat.ioMainnet endpoint for public

/v2/inscribe/order/{orderId}/refund-estimate

RefundAmount = PaidAmount - RefundTxSize \* RefundFeeRate. This value must be greater than SafeRefundAmount, otherwise there's a risk of losing inscriptions used for payment due to errors.

Authorizations

bearerAuth

AuthorizationstringRequired

Bearer authentication header of the form Bearer <token>.

Path parameters

orderIdstringRequired

Responses

200

Successful operation

application/json

codeinteger · enumOptionalPossible values: `0``-1`

msgstringOptionalExample: `OK`

dataobjectOptional

Show properties

401

Invalid API Key

post/v2/inscribe/order/{orderId}/refund-estimate

HTTP

Copy

    POST /v2/inscribe/order/{orderId}/refund-estimate HTTP/1.1
    Host: open-api.unisat.io
    Authorization: Bearer YOUR_SECRET_TOKEN
    Accept: */*
    

Test it

200

Successful operation

Copy

    {
      "code": 0,
      "msg": "OK",
      "data": {
        "paidAmount": 1,
        "refundTxSize": 1,
        "safeRefundAmount": 1
      }
    }

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://docs.unisat.io/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://docs.unisat.io/developer-support/open-api-documentation/api-for-fractal-bitcoin/unisat-inscribe-on-fractal/refund-estimate.md)
.

Copy

On this page

### Estimate the size of the refund transaction[](https://docs.unisat.io/developer-support/open-api-documentation/api-for-fractal-bitcoin/unisat-inscribe-on-fractal/refund-estimate#post-v2-inscribe-order-orderid-refund-estimate)

post

https://open-api-fractal.unisat.ioMainnet endpoint for public.

/v2/inscribe/order/{orderId}/refund-estimate

RefundAmount = PaidAmount - RefundTxSize \* RefundFeeRate. This value must be greater than SafeRefundAmount, otherwise there's a risk of losing inscriptions used for payment due to errors.

Authorizations

bearerAuth

AuthorizationstringRequired

Bearer authentication header of the form Bearer <token>.

Path parameters

orderIdstringRequired

Responses

200

Successful operation

application/json

codeinteger · enumOptionalPossible values: `0``-1`

msgstringOptionalExample: `OK`

dataobjectOptional

Show properties

401

Invalid API Key

post/v2/inscribe/order/{orderId}/refund-estimate

HTTP

Copy

    POST /v2/inscribe/order/{orderId}/refund-estimate HTTP/1.1
    Host: open-api-fractal.unisat.io
    Authorization: Bearer YOUR_SECRET_TOKEN
    Accept: */*
    

Test it

200

Successful operation

Copy

    {
      "code": 0,
      "msg": "OK",
      "data": {
        "paidAmount": 1,
        "refundTxSize": 1,
        "safeRefundAmount": 1
      }
    }

# Getting Started with FairPool | UniSat

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://docs.unisat.io/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://docs.unisat.io/misc/deprecated/fairpool/getting-started-with-fairpool.md)
.

### **Step-by-Step Tutorial**[](https://docs.unisat.io/misc/deprecated/fairpool/getting-started-with-fairpool#step-by-step-tutorial)

**Step 1: Register an Account**

*   Visit [https://www.fairpool.network](https://www.fairpool.network/)
     and click on the "Register" button.
    
*   Fill in your username (Lowercase only) and password. It's recommended that you save this information for future reference.
    
*   You’ll also need to provide a Bitcoin address during registration. This is where your mining earnings will be sent, but don’t worry—you can update this address later if needed.
    

![](https://docs.unisat.io/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3523236551-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FJ4NHAHIVnWQiEecvs1By%252Fuploads%252F6OHwxmTInGWmFn33A6gr%252F2.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D2c26af4e-cbb6-4806-a9fa-1ebc94a17442&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=604f639c979dc06218eeb054433a2a61&sv=3)

**Step 2: Configure your mining equipment settings**

Now that your account is set up, it’s time to configure your miners to connect to FairPool:

*   Go to the homepage of FairPool and find the "Mining Address" section.
    
    *   For Fractal Mainnet - Copy the mining pool address: `stratum+tcp://pool-solo.fairpool.network:3333`
        
    *   For Fractal Testnet - Copy the mining pool address: `stratum+tcp://pool-solo-t1.fairpool.network:3333`
        
    

In your mining equipment’s settings, input the following details:

*   **Pool Address for Mainnet**: `pool-solo.fairpool.network:3333`
    
*   **Pool Address for Testnet:** `pool-solo-t1.fairpool.network:3333`
    
*   **Username:** Name your miner in the format `user_name.worker_name`, where `user_name` is the username you registered with in the first step, for example: `alice.x01`(Lowercase only) . The `worker_name` is the name of your mining machine, but this is not mandatory.
    
*   **Password:** Leave this field empty; it’s not required.
    

After connecting, you should see your miners’ real-time hashrate displayed on FairPool’s homepage, confirming that you’re successfully mining.

![](https://docs.unisat.io/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3523236551-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FJ4NHAHIVnWQiEecvs1By%252Fuploads%252F40UpSn29UnjNwGtgNVRa%252F7.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dbcfb2abc-f033-4600-acb2-bee2d8ab2048&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=92e3f9833cc0445094893b2106456e33&sv=3)

**Step 3: Monitor and Receive Your Earnings**

Once your miners are up and running, here’s how you’ll receive your earnings:

*   **Daily Settlement:** Your earnings are calculated and finalized daily at 00:00 (UTC).
    
*   **Automatic Payouts:** At 06:00 (UTC) per day, your earnings will be automatically sent to the Bitcoin address you registered with.
    
*   **Check Your Balance:** You can log in to your FairPool account anytime to check your current balance and review your earnings history.
    

![](https://docs.unisat.io/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3523236551-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FJ4NHAHIVnWQiEecvs1By%252Fuploads%252FyxqbGBMANeun766Vxdn0%252F8.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D63849e91-4298-4401-bfe3-cf9d47a49c8a&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=9c57e3f1d26dbbe36660d24bb8f26c0a&sv=3)

Last updated 1 year ago

# Unknown

\> For the complete documentation index, see \[llms.txt\](https://docs.unisat.io/llms.txt). Markdown versions of documentation pages are available by appending \`.md\` to page URLs; this page is available as \[Markdown\](https://docs.unisat.io/products/unisat-wallet/address-types.md).

# Address Types

UniSat Wallet supports 4 Bitcoin address formats and allows switching between them in the settings.<br>

| Type          | Script Type | Derivation  | BIP                                                                        |
| ------------- | ----------- | ----------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Native Segwit | P2WPKH      | m/84'/0'/0' | \[bip-0084\](https://github.com/bitcoin/bips/blob/master/bip-0084.mediawiki) |
| Nested Segwit | P2SH-P2WPKH | m/49'/0'/0' | \[bip-0141\](https://github.com/bitcoin/bips/blob/master/bip-0141.mediawiki) |
| Taproot       | P2TR        | m/86'/0'/0' | \[bip-0341\](https://github.com/bitcoin/bips/blob/master/bip-0341.mediawiki) |
| Legacy        | P2PKH       | m/44'/0'/0' |                                                                            |

# Full Node Configuration | UniSat

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://docs.unisat.io/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://docs.unisat.io/misc/deprecated/full-node-configuration.md)
.

In the blockchain technology, Bitcoin full nodes play a crucial role in maintaining the network's security, integrity, and transparency.

#### What is a Full Node?[](https://docs.unisat.io/misc/deprecated/full-node-configuration#what-is-a-full-node)

A full node is a program that independently verifies all transactions and blocks in the Bitcoin network, enforcing every rule in the Bitcoin protocol. Beyond just keeping a complete copy of the blockchain, full nodes contribute to network security by receiving, validating, and relaying transactions and blocks to other nodes.Most full nodes also assist lightweight (SPV) clients by broadcasting their transactions and notifying them of relevant activity in their wallets. Without enough full nodes providing these services, SPV clients would need to rely on centralized services to connect to the network, potentially compromising the decentralized nature of Bitcoin.

#### Why Full Nodes Matter[](https://docs.unisat.io/misc/deprecated/full-node-configuration#why-full-nodes-matter)

1.  Verification of Transactions: Full nodes validate all transactions and blocks, ensuring that only valid transactions are added to the blockchain.
    
2.  Network Security: Each full node independently verifies and cross-checks data, which prevents tampering and ensures that no fraudulent transactions are allowed.
    
3.  Decentralization: Full nodes prevent reliance on central authorities. Since anyone can run a full node, control over Bitcoin remains distributed and open.
    
4.  Privacy Protection: Full nodes don’t require trust in third-party servers, allowing users to interact with the Bitcoin network privately and directly.
    

#### Recommended System Requirements for Running a Full Node[](https://docs.unisat.io/misc/deprecated/full-node-configuration#recommended-system-requirements-for-running-a-full-node)

For optimal performance and long-term stability, the following hardware specifications are recommended for a Bitcoin full node:

Memory (RAM): 8 GB

Disk space:

*   Mainnet: 800 GB
    
*   Testnet: 300 GB
    

Processor (CPU): 2 cores

While it's possible to run a full node on lower specifications, these recommended resources will help ensure that your node operates smoothly and can keep up with the Bitcoin network’s growth.

For more information on how to set up and operate a full node, you can refer to [https://github.com/fractal-bitcoin/fractald-release](https://github.com/fractal-bitcoin/fractald-release)

Last updated 1 year ago

# Single-Step Transfer (brc-20) | UniSat

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://docs.unisat.io/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://docs.unisat.io/misc/articles/single-step-transfer-brc-20.md)
.

**What is Single-Step Transfer?**

Simply put, Single-Step Transfer combines what used to be a two-step process — inscribing a transfer and sending it — into a single, seamless action.

❌ Inscribe transfer inscription > send brc-20

✅ Send brc-20 in ONE STEP!

* * *

**Simplified Transfer Process**

Previously, if you wanted to transfer brc-20 assets (e.g., 100 ORDI) from Address A to Address B:

*   You had to inscribe a transfer, creating a Transfer inscription that locks part of Address A’s available balance into a transferable balance.
    
*   Only after that, you could send your ORDI to Address B, enabling B to receive the assets.
    

Two steps, two transactions, higher fees.

![](https://docs.unisat.io/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3523236551-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FJ4NHAHIVnWQiEecvs1By%252Fuploads%252F9S4WXpaJBvgC1UkVofVp%252Fimage%2520%2823%29.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D07ef6169-aaeb-4c02-b558-7748bbc6f83d&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=02ad91ad27c44c107673d5a840a0770b&sv=3)

* * *

**Now with Single-Step Transfer:**

On the Send page, simply enter the recipient's address and the amount you want to send — just like sending FB.

One step, one transaction — fast and simple!

![](https://docs.unisat.io/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3523236551-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FJ4NHAHIVnWQiEecvs1By%252Fuploads%252FAT98QvyLI52rQdQfin72%252Fimage%2520%2824%29.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dc23651df-7b47-4ed6-86d0-cce7e36489db&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=237a064170b4a0ec75f9d3b02245ec49&sv=3)

* * *

**Why It Matters:**

*   Cuts Costs: By combining two transactions into one, you pay significantly lower fees, making brc-20 transfers more affordable.
    
*   Saves Time: Skip the tedious step of creating a Transfer inscription. Enter the recipient’s address, set the amount, and send—done!
    
*   Streamlines Experience: Simplifies the process when trading brc-20 in a DEX like InSwap or in marketplace.
    
*   Batch transfers: Future updates will allow transferring to up to 1,000 addresses in a single transaction with multiple single-step transfers.
    

* * *

**New Features with Single-Step Transfer:**

**🔸 Signed Mint Inscriptions:** Inscribe mint operations using the Single-Step Transfer method - directly inscribe into OP\_RETURN to prevent creating useless UTXOs at your address.

**🔸 Batch inscribing for Single-Step Transfers (deploy/mint/traditional transfers remain individual)**, and allows spending from UTXOs carrying useless inscriptions to reduce waste and improve efficiency.

* * *

**Why Launch on Fractal First?**

As a Bitcoin-native extension, Fractal resembles Bitcoin closely in its design and adopts the Bitcoin core code, making it the perfect testing ground for upgrades before Bitcoin mainnet deployment.

Our approach to upgrades has always been to remain optimistic about innovations while maintaining steady, careful rollouts to safeguard network integrity and protect user interests. We anticipate that real-world usage on Fractal will help developers to harness the real potential of these brc-20 upgrades.

Fractal’s Highlights:

*   30-second block time — 20× faster than Bitcoin — enabling quicker confirmations and much shorter transaction wait times.
    
*   Perfect for fast-trading scenarios, such as swapping brc-20 assets on InSwap!
    

* * *

**What's Next?**

*   brc-20 Single-Step Transfer now live on Fractal Mainnet. Support will gradually roll out across UniSat services.
    
*   We will closely monitor its performance on Fractal network and continue to advocate for its deployment on Bitcoin mainnet.

*   **Official Website :** [https://www.fairpool.network](https://www.fairpool.network/)
    

FairPool is here to power long-term development and testing on Fractal, with a focus on boosting Fractal’s network connectivity and driving key improvements.

### **Earnings Information**[](https://docs.unisat.io/misc/deprecated/fairpool#earnings-information)

*   **Earnings Distribution Model:** PPLNS (Pay Per Last N Shares)
    
*   **Payment Method:** Automatic daily payouts
    
*   **Payment Time:** 06:00 (UTC)
    
*   **Minimum Payout:** 0.0001 FB
    
*   **Service Fee:** 2%

# FAQ | UniSat

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://docs.unisat.io/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://docs.unisat.io/developer-support/faq.md)
.

Q: How can I begin using UniSat API?

You can visit the Developer Center on the UniSat website and register to instantly get a free API key with a monthly quota of 30,000 calls. For higher limits for personal or commercial projects, you can upgrade to a paid plan. See [How to Acquire a UniSat API Key](https://docs.unisat.io/dev-center/developer-center/how-to-acquire-a-unisat-api-key)
 for more details.

* * *

Q: What is the UniSat API for?

UniSat API is the first brc20 indexer API provider and is also used by leading exchanges. It supports queries for Bitcoin and Fractal Bitcoin blockchain info, balances, transaction histories, and UTXO-related data. It also provides comprehensive indexer data for asset protocols such as brc20, Runes, Alkanes, including balances, holder addresses, and transaction histories.

In addition, UniSat API supports inscription-related data and on-chain activity, enabling developers to quickly deploy inscription minting services. Users can also access UniSat products through the API—including inscribe service and trading products such as Marketplace and InSwap—to mint and trade assets programmatically.

* * *

Q: Is Fractal Bitcoin's API completely free, and what can it be used for?

Fractal Bitcoin’s API is completely free, and its data and API interface structure are fully identical to Bitcoin’s. When developers or projects find that API request costs on the Bitcoin mainnet are prohibitively high during development and testing, they can switch to Fractal and complete their testing there at relatively low cost, then later move to commercial paid APIs on the mainnet once everything is ready.

* * *

UniSat offers four API plans designed for different use cases:

•Free: Ideal for research, experimentation, and high-load testing. This plan provides a one-time API key, making it suitable for short-term projects or initial exploration.

•Specialist: Best for developers running larger scripts and blockchain startups with a growing user base. It’s also a good fit for certain arbitrage traders operating on the Bitcoin network.

•Enterprise: Designed for wallets, exchanges, and startups in their growth stage. This plan is also well suited for market makers running high-frequency strategies across the Bitcoin network and centralized exchanges.

•Custom API: If the request limits or rate limits of the standard plans do not meet your development or trading strategy requirements, please contact the UniSat team to discuss a customized API solution tailored to your needs.

* * *

Q: What are the rate limits for UniSat API?

The UniSat Free and Specialist plans have a rate limit of 5 requests per second, while the Enterprise plan allows 10 requests per second. For higher throughput, custom Enterprise plans are available—top-tier exchanges can get up to 500 requests per second. Contact the UniSat team to discuss your needs.

* * *

Q: What if my API usage exceeds my current plan, and will I lose the remaining value if I upgrade?

If your usage exceeds your current plan, you can either purchase additional usage through a "pay-as-you-go" package or upgrade to a higher-tier plan. When upgrading, the remaining value of your current plan is calculated down to the minute and applied as a credit toward the cost of the new plan.

* * *

Q: Is it possible to support BRC2.0 API data?

Yes. In-module balance queries will be added on top of the existing brc-20 API.

* * *

Q: Can I query UTXOs at low fee rates?

Yes. The API supports UTXO queries in <1 sat/vB mode, returning up to 500 UTXOs per request.

* * *

Q: Can I use the API for commercial projects?

Yes, the UniSat API is designed to support both personal and commercial projects. For commercial use, the Enterprise plan offers the most comprehensive access, including custom call limits and commercial licensing options.

* * *

Q: What payment options are available?

You can pay via PayPal, BTC, or USTD(TRON/ETH). BTC payments are screened through our on-chain KYT system, and any high-risk funds will be returned.

* * *

Q: Is the API subscription auto-renewed?

After purchasing a plan, users can manually enable auto-renewal on their account page. Auto-renewal can also be canceled at any time. Please note that payments are non-refundable, and we do not provide refunds for services that have already been paid for.

Last updated 1 month ago

# UniSat Wallet

### \*\*UniSat Wallet — Home for Your Bitcoin Assets\*\*

\*Easy and safe to trade, inscribe, and manage assets with UniSat\*

UniSat wallet is the first fully open-source, non-custodial, and decentralized wallet built for Bitcoin’s innovative standards. it provides users with a decentralized, self-custody experience alongside a suite of integrated tools, including:

\* UniSat Marketplace
\* UniSat Inscribe
\* InSwap
\* UTXO Management Tool
\* DApp Center

Together, these services deliver a one-stop, seamless on-chain experience for Bitcoin and Fractal Bitcoin. &#x20;

#### \*\*Key Advantages of UniSat Wallet:\*\*

\* \*\*Decentralized\*\*/\*\*Non-Custodial\*\* \*\*Control:\*\* Your private keys, passwords, and seed phrases are never stored by UniSat. Users retain full control of their keys with no personal data collected.
\* \*\*100% Open Source:\*\* All code is available on GitHub, ensuring transparency, security, and community-driven development.
\* \*\*Encryption & Security:\*\* Private keys are encrypted locally with your password, providing robust protection for your assets.
\* \*\*Privacy First:\*\* UniSat does not track personal data. You can import private keys as "imported" accounts for flexible wallet management.
\* \*\*All-in-One Bitcoin & Fractal:\*\* UniSat Wallet supports Native SegWit, Taproot, Nested SegWit, and Legacy address types. You can manage BTC, Ordinals, Runes, and Alkanes in one place, with multi-seed import and batch address derivation. Seamlessly access UniSat Marketplace, Inscribe, InSwap, UniScan and other services, and switch between Bitcoin and Fractal with a single click.
\* \*\*Multi-Platform Support:\*\* Available on iOS, Android, and web browsers for convenient, cross-device access.

Being a non-custodial wallet means you will be in full control of managing and safeguarding your assets, including your seed phrase, private keys, and wallet password. You are the only one with access, and thus fully accountable for their security. You will need:

\* Back up your recovery phrase or private keys securely. Losing them means losing your assets permanently.
\* Set a strong wallet password and keep it safe. UniSat cannot help recover forgotten passwords.
\* Only download UniSat Wallet from the \[official website\](https://unisat.io/download) to avoid fraud or phishing.
\* Be cautious when clicking links or downloading software from any source.

Stay safe and enjoy your journey with UniSat Wallet!

# Transfer brc-20 in UniSat Wallet (2023) | UniSat

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://docs.unisat.io/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://docs.unisat.io/misc/articles/transfer-brc-20-in-unisat-wallet-2023.md)
.

UniSat Wallet now supports TRANSFER of brc-20 since 1.1.15. (available here: [Google Chrome Store](https://chrome.google.com/webstore/detail/ppbibelpcjmhbdihakflkdcoccbgbkpo)
)

Please follow the step-by-step guide below to learn how to transfer your brc-20 to others.

### 1\. Make sure the version of Unisat is the latest.[](https://docs.unisat.io/misc/articles/transfer-brc-20-in-unisat-wallet-2023#id-3af9)

*   Switch to the 'Settings' tab and scroll to the bottom.
    
*   Verify the version number to make sure it’s the latest, current is **1.4.5 .**
    

![](https://docs.unisat.io/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3523236551-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FJ4NHAHIVnWQiEecvs1By%252Fuploads%252FcuPiVtMDN31DytmNmW49%252F1.PNG%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dbde17c96-9d6e-4a9b-9703-05013252b54e&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=fdaceb3a10beadf97c4db6c27c98e0ae&sv=3)

### 2\. Switch to the ‘Home’ tab and click the ‘brc-20’ tag[](https://docs.unisat.io/misc/articles/transfer-brc-20-in-unisat-wallet-2023#id-6bcf)

Each volumn here represents a specific brc-20 tick (like ‘sats’), containing multiple associated inscriptions.Please note that the balance contains two parts:

*   **Transferable**: The amount here has been inscribed into one or more TRANSFER inscriptions (can be transferred immediately).
    
*   **Available**: The amount here is available as a part of your balance (including both minted and received) **but not transferable yet**. To spend it, you must first inscribe a certain amount into a TRANSFER inscription.
    

![](https://docs.unisat.io/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3523236551-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FJ4NHAHIVnWQiEecvs1By%252Fuploads%252FOJRNNpJgCGwt06cLYW2C%252F2.PNG%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D0deccf4e-0183-4c3d-9149-50265e49ad18&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=944875520a1bf9b76340397ea3c567b1&sv=3)

### 3\. Click one of your brc-20s to check out the details.[](https://docs.unisat.io/misc/articles/transfer-brc-20-in-unisat-wallet-2023#e624)

*   All your associated inscriptions are listed here.
    
*   The green ones are transferable (please note we have no transferable inscription yet on the screenshot below).
    

![](https://docs.unisat.io/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3523236551-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FJ4NHAHIVnWQiEecvs1By%252Fuploads%252F7CPypJ1ssguvIbGMHW5c%252F3.PNG%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Deb37627e-2234-4998-b57a-0a52237e22e3&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=d2c72b57f51a8e2e71d835fe7d846df7&sv=3)

### 4\. Click ‘TRANSFER’ to enter the transfer process.[](https://docs.unisat.io/misc/articles/transfer-brc-20-in-unisat-wallet-2023#id-33b2)

*   All transferable inscriptions are listed here for you to choose from.
    
*   The transfer Amount is not an editable field. It can only be calculated by the sum of the chosen amounts of TRANSFER inscriptions.
    
*   If you don’t have enough transferable inscriptions yet, you are going to inscribe them first.
    

![](https://docs.unisat.io/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3523236551-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FJ4NHAHIVnWQiEecvs1By%252Fuploads%252FKBjawFIcRl3jn9ic2tsQ%252F5.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Deb9dc8e6-3299-42ca-beac-2303a138833b&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=bd10756ff2c2c582f645bf73247ba335&sv=3)

### 5\. Click the ‘Inscribe TRANSFER’ button.[](https://docs.unisat.io/misc/articles/transfer-brc-20-in-unisat-wallet-2023#id-85eb)

*   Input the amount that you want to inscribe into a TRANSFER inscription.
    
*   Choose an appropriate fee rate.
    
*   And click the ‘Inscribe TRANSFER’ button on the bottom.
    

![](https://docs.unisat.io/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3523236551-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FJ4NHAHIVnWQiEecvs1By%252Fuploads%252FvmTTTK9K0rNLmwfHNCfn%252F6.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D8157dae1-6446-4ac8-bc73-0ec89e9e7f48&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=5a8f8f0c19003bdc7a6a0a725ddb0678&sv=3)

### 6\. Preview the details of the TRANSFER inscription and click ‘Pay & Inscribe’ to execute[](https://docs.unisat.io/misc/articles/transfer-brc-20-in-unisat-wallet-2023#e3b8)

*   You may return to the earlier steps by clicking the back button and changing values and settings before submitting.
    

![](https://docs.unisat.io/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3523236551-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FJ4NHAHIVnWQiEecvs1By%252Fuploads%252FHdnT6vYYn3AOF6Tk9Fif%252F7.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dd114b993-d461-40e6-90f0-52704a826d7d&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=9b2ca182ae75e15f8c6aae9451fe6188&sv=3)

Preview TRANSFER

![](https://docs.unisat.io/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3523236551-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FJ4NHAHIVnWQiEecvs1By%252Fuploads%252FZTZlJCXy7PyxltvRI3iX%252F8.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D3c9ca91c-24aa-4df9-8364-a641452bb39b&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=a8b786c4eb6faffed384167076f591a6&sv=3)

Sign & Pay for transfer

### 7\. Choose a TRANSFER inscription and proceed to step 2[](https://docs.unisat.io/misc/articles/transfer-brc-20-in-unisat-wallet-2023#a257)

*   Once a number of TRANSFER inscriptions are chosen, we can proceed to send them to the receiver by typing its address (or .sats name)
    

![](https://docs.unisat.io/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3523236551-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FJ4NHAHIVnWQiEecvs1By%252Fuploads%252F3Beh3SQQipQEuJT39IYw%252F9.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dc37edd8e-65d2-46b8-b0f4-809d5f62f382&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=bfd0bde58db87e9b32a7181cf5dbd015&sv=3)

Choose TRANSFER and click ‘Next’

![](https://docs.unisat.io/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3523236551-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FJ4NHAHIVnWQiEecvs1By%252Fuploads%252FLkY4K9e7mNRpJGvyzSM9%252F10.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D3993f5b0-0633-4b2f-9b26-49cb6656743a&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=e4d93c4fe1aa351d8143b42f2421469d&sv=3)

Input the address or .unisat name of the receiver

### 8\. Preview the transaction before sending.[](https://docs.unisat.io/misc/articles/transfer-brc-20-in-unisat-wallet-2023#id-0c70)

*   Click ‘Sign & Send’ to perform the transfer operation eventually.
    

![](https://docs.unisat.io/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3523236551-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FJ4NHAHIVnWQiEecvs1By%252Fuploads%252F5ewMLf4n28ByeTlavhjq%252F11.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dde494cd8-210b-4da0-a86c-4ad297539523&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=db1aee4c7bf5e1813c27a83dc2c26491&sv=3)

Preview the transaction before sending

### 9\. Congratulations! The transfer is now completed![](https://docs.unisat.io/misc/articles/transfer-brc-20-in-unisat-wallet-2023#id-2164)

![](https://docs.unisat.io/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3523236551-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FJ4NHAHIVnWQiEecvs1By%252Fuploads%252FwokyEoKz56iRREpVSJqs%252F12.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D208ea52b-d448-4807-ad53-57ec56ce1f07&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=28b5767293deff797ab133ac5434ebc3&sv=3)

*   UniSat Wallet: [https://unisat.io/](https://unisat.io/)
    
*   brc20 spec: [https://domo-2.gitbook.io/brc-20-experiment/](https://domo-2.gitbook.io/brc-20-experiment/)
    

If you have questions, just ask on our Discord server: [https://discord.gg/EMskB2sMz8](https://discord.gg/EMskB2sMz8)

Happy inscribing and transferring!

UniSat Team (2024–09–02)

# Sub 1 sat/vB Mode Setup Guide | UniSat

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://docs.unisat.io/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://docs.unisat.io/misc/articles/understanding-sub-1-sat-vb-bitcoin-transactions/sub-1-sat-vb-mode-setup-guide.md)
.

### 1\. What is Sub 1 sat/vB Mode ?[](https://docs.unisat.io/misc/articles/understanding-sub-1-sat-vb-bitcoin-transactions/sub-1-sat-vb-mode-setup-guide#id-1.-what-is-sub-1-sat-vb-mode)

This refers to selecting a fee rate lower than 1 sat/vB when inscribing or using UTXO management features. Most network nodes enforce a default relay policy requiring ≥1 sat/vB. But **Sub 1 sat/vB transactions is more common now that network fee is generally low.**

✨ **Advantage of Sub 1 sat/vB Mode**:

Save transaction fees, suitable for non-urgent or large batch minting.

⚠️ **Risk of Sub 1 sat/vB Mode**:

Most pools _**do not**_ mine sub **1 sat/vB transactions.** Confirmation is **not guaranteed** may take a long time (from several hours to several days) or never get processed.

**‼️ Check Current Network Fee Rate**

Before setting sub 1 sat/vB fee rate, we recommend checking the current Bitcoin network congestion. A fee rate of 1 sat/vB or lower means it may result in longer confirmation times.

You may use [mempool.space](https://mempool.space/)
 to view the fee rate and estimated confirmation time in real time.

![](https://docs.unisat.io/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3523236551-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FJ4NHAHIVnWQiEecvs1By%252Fuploads%252FZCvxNNwzRMwcgwm32SOV%252F1.PNG%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D061e83d8-eb62-40c9-b05e-15ac81b15728&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=bfcdabf17c86d3e504ff2458102573f8&sv=3)

1.  ### How to Set **Sub 1 sat/vB Fee** Order[](https://docs.unisat.io/misc/articles/understanding-sub-1-sat-vb-bitcoin-transactions/sub-1-sat-vb-mode-setup-guide#how-to-set-sub-1-sat-vb-fee-order)
    

Currently, UniSat Inscribe and UTXO Management Tool services have supported **sub 1 sat/vB mode**, allowing users to set fee rates below 1 sat/vB to reduce inscribing costs.

*   In the fee selection area, set your custom fee rate manually or use the slider, e.g., `0.2 sat/vB`.
    

![](https://docs.unisat.io/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3523236551-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FJ4NHAHIVnWQiEecvs1By%252Fuploads%252FAI0Iek3qcZ3h1QcxqBMw%252F22.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Df9478a19-5359-410e-b36d-76ede472f96c&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=47afe973355bdbc0e53ac5f9c9b8a162&sv=3)

**Acknowledge Risk Warning**

*   The system will display a risk alert when enabling sub 1 sat/vB fee mode. Please read the on-page warning carefully, and confirm that you fully understand the risks.
    

![](https://docs.unisat.io/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3523236551-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FJ4NHAHIVnWQiEecvs1By%252Fuploads%252FOJv2iG0I01uh9wDyPjA7%252F33.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D6c77f3b8-dea0-4e48-b31c-3ea6628151a0&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=946895120f3dfc5886b03b9ec17f2d5d&sv=3)

**Submit the Order or transaction**

*   Click **Submit** to complete the process.
    

1.  ### Difference Between Payment and Minting Transactions (Important)[](https://docs.unisat.io/misc/articles/understanding-sub-1-sat-vb-bitcoin-transactions/sub-1-sat-vb-mode-setup-guide#difference-between-payment-and-minting-transactions-important)
    

Please note:

*   **Under Sub 1 sat/vB Mode, you are only adjusting the fee rate for minting transaction, not for the payment transaction.**
    
*   Please **do not** manually change the fee rate for payment to ensure quick order submission
    

![](https://docs.unisat.io/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3523236551-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FJ4NHAHIVnWQiEecvs1By%252Fuploads%252FG436OOJ4t7zR4icbpMrX%252F5.PNG%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dcd4b66ca-b2d4-49fd-9ac2-33279d712d9d&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=d9cb19a9550f2a3f9132affcc0fc362b&sv=3)

# UniSat Game Framework | UniSat

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://docs.unisat.io/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://docs.unisat.io/developer-support/open-api-documentation/unisat-game-framework.md)
.

The advent of blockchain technology has revolutionized the gaming industry, offering unprecedented opportunities for innovation and monetization. At UniSat, we provide robust tools and APIs to harness the power of Bitcoin blockchain for game development. This guide will walk you through using UniSat Inscribe API, Marketplace API, and more to create Bitcoin games.

*   **How to Inscribe brc-20 FTs and Ordinals NFTs Using UniSat Inscribe API?**
    

This guide provides detailed steps on using the UniSat Inscribe API to mint brc-20 Fungible Tokens and Ordinals Non-Fungible Tokens.

You can implement this process through our interface available at: [https://docs.unisat.io/dev/unisat-developer-service/unisat-inscribe](https://docs.unisat.io/dev/unisat-developer-service/unisat-inscribe)

*   **How to Access brc-20 Market and Ordinals NFTs Marketplace Using UniSat Marketplace API?**
    

To engage players in trading in-game assets, integrate your game with the UniSat Marketplace. Our API makes it seamless to connect with both brc-20 market and Ordinals NFT Marketplace.

You can attempt to integrate it using the following interface: [https://docs.unisat.io/dev/unisat-developer-service/unisat-marketplace](https://docs.unisat.io/dev/unisat-developer-service/unisat-marketplace)

*   **How to Create Reusable On-Chain Game Assets Using Recursive Inscriptions?**
    

Recursive inscriptions are fundamentally composed of HTML and SVG, allowing for resource reuse simply by having the inscription content point to a relevant link.

For reference, see: [https://ordinals.com/inscription/7f364014338d6dff53557244182033f84f2a7de46a89685aa0eec725317de216i0](https://ordinals.com/inscription/7f364014338d6dff53557244182033f84f2a7de46a89685aa0eec725317de216i0)

*   **How to Develop brc-20 GameFi Contract Inscriptions Similar to brc20-swap?**
    

Create an independently executable JavaScript file that operates on-chain, and execute it using a VM2 Virtual Machine. Here's a simple example：

Copy

    const contractText = await api.inscriptionContent(inscriptionId);
    const vm = new NodeVM({
        require: {
          external: true,
          root: "./",
        },
      });
    const bundle = vm.run(contractText, "vm.js");

# The Journey of a Bitcoin Alchemist | UniSat

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://docs.unisat.io/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://docs.unisat.io/misc/events/the-journey-of-a-bitcoin-alchemist.md)
.

Enrol. Study. Ascend.

Track your training progress here: [https://unisat.io/address/my\_title](https://unisat.io/address/my_title)

### **How to play?**[](https://docs.unisat.io/misc/events/the-journey-of-a-bitcoin-alchemist#how-to-play)

#### Step 1: Enrol[](https://docs.unisat.io/misc/events/the-journey-of-a-bitcoin-alchemist#step-1-enrol)

🔮 Start trading Alkanes on UniSat Marketplace and launch your training to become a Bitcoin Alchemist!

#### Step 2: Study[](https://docs.unisat.io/misc/events/the-journey-of-a-bitcoin-alchemist#step-2-study)

🔮 555 out of all traders each week will unlock the weekly achievement. Snapshot taken at 10pm EST every Thursday.

🔮 How to be in the 555? Study smart AND hard- trade the right tokens and volume matters.

🔮 Each weekly snapshot is independent and generates a new set of 555 addresses.

#### Step 3: Ascend[](https://docs.unisat.io/misc/events/the-journey-of-a-bitcoin-alchemist#step-3-ascend)

🔮 Your Bitcoin Alchemist training consists of **12 tiers**:

_Novice → Apprentice → Disciple → Artisan → Savant → Sage → Adept → Master → Philosopher → Archmage → Luminary → Legend✨_

🔮 **Everyone begins at** tier 0 Mundane

🔮 Ascend to higher tiers with consecutive weekly achievements (streaks) ie. one achievement to ascend to Novice from Mundane, and two consecutive achievements from Novice to Apprentice.

🔮 Different tiers require different no. of streaks

### **Rewards?**[](https://docs.unisat.io/misc/events/the-journey-of-a-bitcoin-alchemist#rewards)

*   Weekly: Bitcoin Alchemists can participate in raffles on **UniSat Drops** → [https://unisat.io/drops](https://unisat.io/drops)
    
    *   Rewards usually consist of top trading Alkanes/Orbitals from that week(purchased by UniSat), as well as WL and airdrops spots from projects
        
    
*   Long term? We don’t know yet. Become a legend-tier Alchemist and find out!
    

### **Why This Works**[](https://docs.unisat.io/misc/events/the-journey-of-a-bitcoin-alchemist#why-this-works)

Reward organic actions – not forced trading. We're committed to building a community that truly grows and stays with the ecosystem.

**✨ Timing + Knowledge + Patience = Magic**

# What Happens Below 1 sat/vB + The Hidden Risks of Sub-1 sat/vB Bitcoin Transactions | UniSat

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://docs.unisat.io/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://docs.unisat.io/misc/articles/understanding-sub-1-sat-vb-bitcoin-transactions/what-happens-below-1-sat-vb-+-the-hidden-risks-of-sub-1-sat-vb-bitcoin-transactions.md)
.

#### **What Happens When Bitcoin Fee Rates Drop Below 1 sat/vB?**[](https://docs.unisat.io/misc/articles/understanding-sub-1-sat-vb-bitcoin-transactions/what-happens-below-1-sat-vb-+-the-hidden-risks-of-sub-1-sat-vb-bitcoin-transactions#what-happens-when-bitcoin-fee-rates-drop-below-1-sat-vb)

Bitcoin transaction fees are measured in **satoshis per virtual byte (sat/vB)**. This unit helps miners prioritize transactions when adding blocks to the blockchain. Bitcoin's **default relay policy** (followed by most nodes) requires a minimum fee rate of **1 sat/vB** for a transaction to be relayed across the network. This policy exists to:

1.  Provide a minimum economic incentive for miners to include transactions.
    
2.  Prevent spam by making it costly to flood the network.
    
3.  Maintain efficient network operation by limiting mempool bloat.
    

Transactions paying **less than 1 sat/vB** (sub-1 sat/vB) are considered **non-standard** under this default policy.

However, as the ecosystem evolves there may be some demand for sub-1 sat/vB transactions and more occasions where the miner may accept these transactions such as when the mempool cleared recently.So how do these sub-1 sat/vB transactions work?

* * *

#### **⏳ When Can Sub-1 sat/vB Transactions Be Confirmed?**[](https://docs.unisat.io/misc/articles/understanding-sub-1-sat-vb-bitcoin-transactions/what-happens-below-1-sat-vb-+-the-hidden-risks-of-sub-1-sat-vb-bitcoin-transactions#when-can-sub-1-sat-vb-transactions-be-confirmed)

*   **Not Relayed:** They are **not broadcast or relayed** by nodes enforcing the default policy. They will **not enter the public mempool**.
    
*   **Confirmation only possible** if:
    
    *   The transaction is **submitted directly to a miner** (bypassing the public peer-to-peer network), _or_
        
    *   The transaction is submitted via a **private relay service** (like Slipstream used by some pools) that accepts sub-1 sat/vB rates, _or_
        
    
*   **Not Guaranteed:** Even if directly submitted to a miner or pool, **inclusion is never guaranteed**.
    

Miners prioritize transactions based on their own fee thresholds and strategies. Sub-1 sat/vB transactions are the lowest priority and will only be mined when there is plenty of unused block space and the individual miner or mining pool explicitly chooses to accept and mine sub-1 sat/vB transactions using custom mempool policies.

* * *

#### **💡 Potential Benefits of Sub-1 sat/vB Transactions**[](https://docs.unisat.io/misc/articles/understanding-sub-1-sat-vb-bitcoin-transactions/what-happens-below-1-sat-vb-+-the-hidden-risks-of-sub-1-sat-vb-bitcoin-transactions#potential-benefits-of-sub-1-sat-vb-transactions)

*   **Lower Costs:** _If confirmed_, they enable cheaper transactions, potentially benefiting low-value transfers or microtransactions. (It wouldn’t make sense if you want send $1 worth of BTC but have to spend $5 on gas right)
    
*   **Reduced Minting Costs:** Protocols relying heavily on on-chain transactions for operations like minting (e.g., Runes, Ordinals, brc-20 tickers) could see significant cost reductions.
    

* * *

#### The Hidden Risks of Sub-1 sat/vB Transactions[](https://docs.unisat.io/misc/articles/understanding-sub-1-sat-vb-bitcoin-transactions/what-happens-below-1-sat-vb-+-the-hidden-risks-of-sub-1-sat-vb-bitcoin-transactions#the-hidden-risks-of-sub-1-sat-vb-transactions)

#1 Stuck Transactions:

*   Most mining pools currently **do not mine** transactions with 0.1 sat/vB or similar ultra-low fee rates
    
*   Your transaction may remain **unconfirmed for days, weeks, or indefinitely**
    
*   **Funds become inaccessible** - you can't spend the same UTXOs until the transaction confirms
    
*   **P**articularly problematic for time-sensitive operations such as mint or trade
    

#2 Dust UTXO Attacks

**How the Attack Works:**

*   **Malicious actors** send low-fee-rate UTXOs to your address
    
*   Later, your wallet automatically includes this UTXO when constructing a new transaction
    
*   The **entire transaction inherits the low fee rate**, making it unconfirmable
    
*   You must pay expensive RBF (Replace-By-Fee) acceleration costs to fix the issue
    
*   Why it’s sneaky:
    
    *   Our wallet blocks UTXOs <600 sats, but attacks over 600 sats bypass this.
        
    *   Merging these UTXOs (via wallet tools) spreads the poison to new transactions.
        
    

#3: Transaction Chain Limitations

**The Technical Problem:**

*   Bitcoin's mempool has a **25-transaction ancestor limit**
    
*   Repeatedly spending 0.1 sat/vB transactions quickly approaches this limit
    
*   Once reached, **new transactions cannot be broadcast**
    
*   Regular users have **no way to resolve this** without technical tools
    

**Resolution Challenges:**

*   **Only solution** is using RBF (Replace-By-Fee) acceleration tools to replace earlier transactions
    
*   **Current tool limitations** - many RBF implementations have bugs and don't handle data cleanup properly
    

#4: Increased Spam Transactions:

Lowering the _effective_ cost barrier significantly makes it cheaper for attackers to flood the network with spam or dust transactions (very small outputs), potentially disrupting normal operation.

* * *

#### 🎯 Recommendations for Users[](https://docs.unisat.io/misc/articles/understanding-sub-1-sat-vb-bitcoin-transactions/what-happens-below-1-sat-vb-+-the-hidden-risks-of-sub-1-sat-vb-bitcoin-transactions#recommendations-for-users)

*   Avoid sub-1 sat/vB transactions unless you fully understand the risks
    
*   Be Aware of the limited ecosystem support for handling sub-1 sat/vB transactions properly and potential inconsistent behavior across different wallet implementations
    
*   Keep RBF tools available for emergency acceleration
    
*   We recommend checking the current Bitcoin network congestion. A fee rate of 1 sat/vB or lower means it may result in longer confirmation times. You may use [mempool.space](https://mempool.space/)
     to view the fee rate and estimated confirmation time in real time.
    

![](https://docs.unisat.io/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3523236551-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FJ4NHAHIVnWQiEecvs1By%252Fuploads%252F8cxLuXVdC63zPNw4L3uq%252F88.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D6d40893d-acdc-4c3a-939f-dde0a5efeba1&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=52d6d5e222144062e2b1b5296f5d805d&sv=3)

# How to Participate in BTC Staking on Babylon Using UniSat Wallet | UniSat

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://docs.unisat.io/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://docs.unisat.io/misc/events/babylon-staking-activity/how-to-participate-in-btc-staking-on-babylon-using-unisat-wallet.md)
.

We support you in using UniSat Extension Wallet to participate in Babylon’s BTC staking activities, offering a seamless Bitcoin staking experience. This guide is for reference only. Participation in staking is at the user’s own discretion and responsibility.

**Step 1: Prepare Your UniSat Wallet**

Please ensure that your UniSat Extension Wallet or UniSat App Wallet is updated to the latest version. Click this link to download or update: [https://unisat.io/download](https://unisat.io/download)

**Step 2: Access the Babylon DApp**

*   Switch your network to Bitcoin Mainnet.
    
*   Open the wallet menu at the bottom, click Discover (DApp Center), find Babylon, and click to enter.
    

![](https://docs.unisat.io/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3523236551-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FJ4NHAHIVnWQiEecvs1By%252Fuploads%252FD8PQsKARKUmx9Oo7nlnm%252F1.PNG%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dcb5b0457-0e0c-4489-bf98-99e6f618e81b&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=ef52fadd2bf99ccd65a4c39afc29b8ec&sv=3)

**Step 3: Participate in Babylon Phase 2 Staking**

1.  After entering the Babylon Bitcoin Staking interface, you will see the **Babylon Bitcoin Staking Phase 2** page. Click the **Go to Babylon** button to proceed to the staking page.
    

![](https://docs.unisat.io/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3523236551-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FJ4NHAHIVnWQiEecvs1By%252Fuploads%252FFsHiwFmy0egTREf4QSLr%252F2.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D420bd808-5108-4c77-be04-54c4d5c99a7f&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=89727a12a8d6e4b8a83be9b009332b69&sv=3)

1.  Click the **Connect Wallet** button in the top right corner. You will be prompted to connect your Bitcoin wallet address and your Babylon chain wallet address. Please select your **UniSat Wallet** for both.
    

![](https://docs.unisat.io/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3523236551-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FJ4NHAHIVnWQiEecvs1By%252Fuploads%252FScCKSEWSuABQ2CfUBY58%252F3.PNG%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D1fa0ba11-9e71-4314-922d-a5f2e3546c90&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=591ed2820d0fe7fec648032dc24c7306&sv=3)

![](https://docs.unisat.io/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3523236551-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FJ4NHAHIVnWQiEecvs1By%252Fuploads%252F1lRAZEJUaSnfUYeJjxyZ%252F4.PNG%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Db170cbd1-fd8e-4f83-abc6-b7ce47a8a4bc&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=80703a2301d576d05974c9bbfb652e88&sv=3)

1.  Choose the staking pool you want to participate in, enter the amount of BTC you wish to stake, confirm the amount, and click **Preview** to sign and complete the transaction.
    

![](https://docs.unisat.io/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3523236551-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FJ4NHAHIVnWQiEecvs1By%252Fuploads%252F0i4Ct8VfVoBagMHgntgA%252F5.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D329872a8-dbbb-45bc-8165-1d3cf7943a1b&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=ce9eea916dd0331bd885799b4ae75063&sv=3)

1.  Once your staking is complete, you can view your staking history in the **Staking History** section.
    

![](https://docs.unisat.io/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3523236551-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FJ4NHAHIVnWQiEecvs1By%252Fuploads%252FvoYf2xBtKeDOr5PsRggj%252F6.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D2dfd9469-8e0e-4b4f-b23b-b394119b5267&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=10021dfe659d9259848353b636dc77e6&sv=3)

#### Tips：[](https://docs.unisat.io/misc/events/babylon-staking-activity/how-to-participate-in-btc-staking-on-babylon-using-unisat-wallet#tips)

*   If the Babylon DApp entry is not showing in your UniSat wallet, please ensure your wallet is updated to the latest version.
    
*   If you encounter any technical issues, we recommend contacting Babylon’s official support at https://discord.com/invite/babylonglobal or reaching out to us via our Discord: http://discord.gg/unisat
    
*   Please make sure you fully understand the associated risks and terms before participating in staking.
    

**For more information on Babylon BTC staking, please refer to their official documentation:** [https://docs.babylonlabs.io/guides/overview/bitcoin\_staking/](https://docs.babylonlabs.io/guides/overview/bitcoin_staking/)

# API Version Upgrade completed: Untrack Useless brc20 Inscriptions | UniSat

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://docs.unisat.io/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://docs.unisat.io/developer-support/open-api-documentation/api-version-upgrade-notes/api-version-upgrade-completed-untrack-useless-brc20-inscriptions.md)
.

_Scheduled Deployment: 2025-05-20_

Dear API Users,

We hereby notify you about some changes to our Bitcoin chain API services affecting three endpoints related to BRC20 inscription management. Please review the following updates carefully to ensure service continuity.

#### Affected Endpoints[](https://docs.unisat.io/developer-support/open-api-documentation/api-version-upgrade-notes/api-version-upgrade-completed-untrack-useless-brc20-inscriptions#affected-endpoints)

1.  `/inscription/info/{inscriptionId}`
    
2.  `/utxo/{txid}/{vout}`
    
3.  `/address/{address}/inscription-data`
    

#### Changes[](https://docs.unisat.io/developer-support/open-api-documentation/api-version-upgrade-notes/api-version-upgrade-completed-untrack-useless-brc20-inscriptions#changes)

*   Untrack the useless BRC20 inscriptions：BRC20 inscriptions of type **TRANSFER** (those that have undergone transfers) and **MINT** inscriptions, which no longer hold value, will no longer be available for query after this update.
    

* * *

#### Detailed Interface Changes[](https://docs.unisat.io/developer-support/open-api-documentation/api-version-upgrade-notes/api-version-upgrade-completed-untrack-useless-brc20-inscriptions#detailed-interface-changes)

**1\.** `**/utxo/{txid}/{vout}**`

**Before Upgrade:**

Copy

    
    {
      "code": 0,
      "msg": "ok",
      "data": {
        "txid": "...",
        "vout": 0,
        "satoshi": 546,
        "scriptType": "5120",
        "scriptPk": "...",
        "codeType": 9,
        "address": "...",
        "height": 815539,
        "idx": 2516,
        "isOpInRBF": false,
        "isSpent": false,
        "inscriptionsCount": 2,
        "inscriptions": [\
          {\
            "inscriptionNumber": 38012882,\
            "inscriptionId": "...",\
            "offset": 0,\
            "moved": false,\
            "sequence": 0,\
            "isCursed": false,\
            "isVindicate": false,\
            "isBRC20Ext": false,\
            "isBRC20": true\
          },\
          {\
            "inscriptionNumber": 38012883,\
            "inscriptionId": "....",\
            "offset": 0,\
            "moved": false,\
            "sequence": 0,\
            "isCursed": false,\
            "isVindicate": false,\
            "isBRC20Ext": false,\
            "isBRC20": false\
          }\
        ]
      }
    }

**After Upgrade:**

**Migration Guidance:**

1.  The `inscriptionsCount` (no changes required)
    
2.  The `inscriptions` array will eventually be removed for useless brc20 Inscriptions
    

* * *

**2\.** `**/address/{address}/inscription-data**`

**Before Upgrade:**

**After Upgrade:**

**Key Changes:**

*   All useless BRC20 inscriptions will be removed
    

* * *

**3\.** `**/inscription/info/{inscriptionId}**`

**Before**

**After**

**Critical Update:**When you query a useless brc20 inscription

*   `utxo` object now returns empty strings
    
*   `address`now returns empty string
    

* * *

#### **Test Resources:**[](https://docs.unisat.io/developer-support/open-api-documentation/api-version-upgrade-notes/api-version-upgrade-completed-untrack-useless-brc20-inscriptions#test-resources)

Testnet4 has been upgraded and can be tested.

[![Logo](https://docs.unisat.io/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fopen-api-testnet4.unisat.io%2Ffavicon-32x32.png&width=20&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=f6d6bb0a64fc0a8873a77968dff317c8&sv=3)Swagger UIopen-api-testnet4.unisat.io](https://open-api-testnet4.unisat.io/swagger.html)

Feel free to let us know if you have any questions or concerns.
